Toward the endoscopic classification of unipotent representations of p p -adic G 2 G_{2} Thanks: Clifton Cunningham gratefully acknowledges the support of NSERC Discovery Grant RGPIN-2020-05220 Thanks: Andrew Fiori thanks and acknowledges both the University of Lethbridge for their financial support as well as the support of NSERC Discovery Grant RGPIN-2020-05316. Thanks: Qing Zhang would like to thank the support NSFC grant 11801577, a postdoc fellowship from the Pacific Institute for the Mathematical Sciences (PIMS)
Clifton Cunningham, Andrew Fiori, Qing Zhang

Abstract
. We begin this paper by reviewing the Langlands correspondence for unipotent representations of the exceptional group of type G 2 G_{2} over a p p -adic field F F and present it in an explicit form. Then we compute all ABV-packets, as defined in [ 9 ] following ideas from Vogan’s 1993 paper The local Langlands Conjecture , and prove that these packets satisfy properties derived from the expectation that they are generalized A-packets. We attach distributions to ABV-packets for G 2 G_{2} and its endoscopic groups and study a geometric endoscopic transfer of these distributions. This paper builds on earlier work by the same authors.



0.
Introduction

0.1.
Background and motivation

Local Arthur packets are finite sets
\Pi_{\psi}(G(F))
of irreducible admissible representations of a
p
-adic
G(F)
,
characterized by endoscopic character identities for symplectic and quasi-split orthogonal groups
G
(and their inner twists over
F
) in
[
3
]
.
Arthur packets generalize tempered
L
-packets and they remedy certain drawbacks of
L
-packets associated with stable distributions.
The description of Arthur packets in
[
3
]
, relies heavily on the trace formula.
These notions have been extended to some other groups, including unitary groups in
[
24
]
,
but not yet to exceptional groups; however, some candidates for local Arthur packets for
G_{2}(F)
were proposed in
[
13
]
,
[
14
]
and
[
15
]
.
In this paper we use the microlocal geometry of the moduli space of unramified Langlands parameters to calculate ABV-packets, as defined in
[
9
]
building on ideas from
[
28
]
, for all unipotent representations of
G_{2}(F)
and show that these packets are compatible with endoscopy in a very precise sense.

Vogan’s work
[
28
]
shows that there are advantages to studying the representation theory of
G(F)
together with that of its pure inner forms
G^{\delta}(F)
simultaneously; in this way one passes from
L
-packets
\Pi_{\phi}(G(F))
to "pure"
L
-packets
\Pi^{\text{pure}}_{\phi}(G)
and from Arthur packets
\Pi_{\psi}(G(F))
to "pure" Arthur packets
\Pi^{\text{pure}}_{\psi}(G)
.
As explained in
[
9
]
, when Arthur’s work is viewed from this perspective it determines a function
\Pi^{\text{pure}}_{\psi}(G)\to\mathsf{Rep}(A_{\psi})
where
A_{\psi}
is the component group of
Z_{{\widehat{G}}}(\psi)
.

A geometric construction of Arthur packets for any
p
-adic group is proposed in
[
9
]
building on ideas introduced by
[
28
]
and also by an adaptation of ideas from
[
1
]
. In fact, this geometric construction is more general: for any Langlands parameter
\phi
for
G(F)
,
[
9
]
constructs a group
A_{\phi}^{{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}}
, and proposes a packet
\Pi_{\phi}^{{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}}(G)
of irreducible representations of
G(F)
and its pure inner forms, called the ABV-packet of
\phi
, and a natural map
\Pi_{\phi}^{{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}}(G)\to\mathsf{Rep}(A_{\phi}^{{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}})
. If
\phi
happens to be of Arthur type, then
A_{\phi}^{{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}}=A_{\psi}
for the associated Arthur parameter
\psi
.
The main conjecture of
[
9
]
says that when
\phi
is of Arthur type then
\Pi_{\phi}^{{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}}(G)=\Pi^{\text{pure}}_{\psi}(G)
and the map
\Pi_{\phi}^{{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}}(G)\to\mathsf{Rep}(A_{\phi}^{{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}})
should be the same as
\Pi^{\text{pure}}_{\psi}(G)\to\mathsf{Rep}(A_{\psi})
when
G
is a classical group, where
\psi
is the associated Arthur parameter. The construction in
[
9
]
proposes a concrete way to compute Arthur packets for classical groups, as illustrated by the numerous examples in
[
9
]
, and also provides a method to generalize Arthur packets to exceptional groups and non-Arthur type local Langlands parameters.

0.2.
Fundamental properties of ABV-packets and packet coefficients

To give some details of our results, we recall that, in
[
23
]
Lusztig provided a version of the local Langlands correspondence for unipotent representations and showed that the unipotent representations are classified by unramified parameters. Using that, in this paper we compute the ABV-packets defined in
[
9
]
, for each unramified local Langlands parameter
\phi
of
G_{2}(F)
, and thus we obtain a decomposition

\Pi(G_{2}(F))_{\text{unip}}=\mathop{\bigcup}\limits_{\phi\in\Phi(G_{2}(F))_{\text{unr}}}\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}(G_{2}(F)),

where
\Pi(G_{2}(F))_{\text{unip}}
is the set of isomorphism classes of irreducible unipotent representations of
G_{2}(F)
. For every ABV-packet
\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}(G_{2}(F))
, the general, canonical, geometric constructions in
[
9
]
also give a group
A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
and a function

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}(G_{2}(F))&\to&\mathsf{Rep}(A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi})\\
\pi&\mapsto&\langle\ ,\pi\rangle\end{array}

and thus a function
\langle\ ,\ \rangle:A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}\times\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}(G_{2}(F))\to{\bar{\mathbb{Q}}}
; we refer to this function as the
ABV-packet coefficients
for
\phi
.

In this paper we argue that ABV-packet coefficients provide a canonical way to extend the notion of Arthur packets to all unipotent representations of the exceptional
p
-adic group
G_{2}
, including those that are not of Arthur type.
For instance, in Theorem
2.2
we show that the ABV-packet coefficients extend the local Langlands correspondence for unipotent representations of
G_{2}(F)
:
for all unramified Langlands parameters
\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G_{2}}}
, the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}(G_{2}(F))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\phi}(G_{2}(F))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{A_{\phi}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LLC}}

where
\widehat{A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}}
(resp.
\widehat{A_{\phi}}
) denotes the set of characters of irreducible representations of
A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
(resp.
A_{\phi}
).
Here the local Langlands correspondence is normalized such that if
\pi\in\Pi_{\phi}(G_{2}(F))
is generic or spherical then the corresponding representation of
A_{\phi}
is trivial.
We give other fundamental properties of ABV-packet coefficients in Theorem
2.2
.
For instance, Part
5
of Theorem
2.2
shows that for every unramified Langlands parameter
\phi
there is an unramified Langlands parameter
{\hat{\phi}}
, with the same infinitesimal parameter as
{\hat{\phi}}
, such that the Aubert involution defines a bijection

\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}(G_{2}(F))\to\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\hat{\phi}}(G_{2}(F)).

0.3.
Distributions attached to ABV-packets

We study distributions defined by ABV-packet coefficients.
To do this we introduce an algebraic group
\mathcal{S}^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
contained in
Z_{{\widehat{G}}}(\phi)
such that
A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}=\pi_{0}(\mathcal{S}^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi})
and
\mathcal{S}^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}=Z_{{\widehat{G_{2}}}}(\psi)
if
\phi
is of Arthur type
\psi
.
For
s\in\mathcal{S}^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
, we set

(1)

\Theta_{\phi,s}{\,:=\,}\sum_{\pi\in\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi_{\psi}}(G_{2}(F))}(-1)^{\dim(\phi)-\dim(\pi)}\langle s,\pi\rangle\ \Theta_{\pi},

as in Definition
4
, where we identify
s
with its image under
\mathcal{S}^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}\to A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
and where
\Theta_{\pi}
is the Harish-Chandra distribution character determined by the admissible representation
\pi
.
The terms
\dim(\phi)
and
\dim(\pi)
are defined in Section
2.2
.
We show that if
\phi
is of Arthur type
\psi
then

(-1)^{\dim(\phi)-\dim(\pi)}\langle s,\pi\rangle=\langle s_{\psi}\,s,\pi\rangle,

where
s_{\psi}
is the image of
\psi(1,-1)\in Z_{{\widehat{G}}_{2}}(\psi)
; in this case, Equation (
1
) takes the more familiar form

\Theta_{\psi,s}=\sum_{\pi\in\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi_{\psi}}(G_{2}(F))}\langle s_{\psi}s,\pi\rangle\ \Theta_{\pi}.

In Theorem
2.14
we prove that if
\pi
is an irreducible unipotent representation of
G_{2}(F)
then
\Theta_{\pi}
can be expressed in terms of the distributions
\Theta_{\phi,s}
, letting
\phi
range over unramified
L
-parameters with the same infinitesimal parameter as
\pi
and letting
s
range over representatives for the components of
\mathcal{S}^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
.
This is an important part of the endoscopic classification promised in the title of this paper.
We also show that the span of the distributions
\Theta_{\phi,s}
, as
s
ranges over
\mathcal{S}^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
, coincides with the span of the distributions
\Theta_{\pi}
, as
\pi
ranges over
\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
, if and only if
\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}(G_{2}(F))\to\widehat{A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}}
is bijective.
In this theorem we also show that if
\Theta_{\phi}{\,:=\,}\Theta_{\phi,1}
is stable when
\phi
is elliptic (see Section
2.10
), then
\Theta_{\phi}
is stable for all
\phi
.

0.4.
Lifting stable distributions from endoscopic groups

We show the ABV-packet coefficients
\langle\ ,\ \rangle
are compatible with the theory of endoscopy in the following sense.
Let
(G,s,\xi)
be an endoscopic triple for
G_{2}
.
Unlike the group
G_{2}
, the endoscopic groups
G
may admit pure inner forms.
Recall that a pure inner form for
G
is a cocycle
\delta\in Z^{1}(F,G)
and that
\delta
determines an inner form
G^{\delta}
of
G
.
We calculate the ABV-packets and ABV-packet coefficients for the endoscopic group
G
.
We generalize the definition of the distributions above Equation (
1
) from
G_{2}
to the pure inner forms of endoscopic groups
G
for
G_{2}
.
In Definition
8
we introduce a "lifting" of stable distributions attached to ABV-packets for
G(F)
to invariant distributions on
G_{2}(F)
and prove Theorem
4.6
:
if
\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G}}
is an unramified Langlands parameter that is
\xi
-conormal, in the sense of Definition
6
, then

(2)

\Lift_{(G,s,\xi)}\Theta^{G}_{\phi}=\Theta_{\xi\circ\phi,s},

for all
s\in\mathcal{S}^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
, for
\mathcal{S}^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
given in Definition
1
.
We show that if
\phi
is of Arthur type then it is always
\xi
-conormal.
Since
G(F)
is also an endoscopic group for the inner form
G^{\delta}(F)
we also define "lifting" from stable distribution attached to ABV-packets for
G(F)
to invariant distributions on
G^{\delta}(F)
and, in Theorem
4.6
, we show that if
\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G}}
is an unramified Langlands parameter that is relevant to
G^{\delta}(F)
, in the sense of
[
6
]
, then the lift of
\Theta^{G}_{\phi}
is
e(\delta)\Theta^{G^{\delta}}_{\phi}
, where
e(\delta)
is the Kottwitz sign.
Conjecture
1
predicts that
\Theta_{\xi\circ\phi,s}
is the Langlands-Shelstad transfer of the stable distribution
\Theta^{G}_{\phi}
.

All this comes together in Theorem
4.7
, in which we show that ABV-packet coefficients are uniquely characterized by the properties given in this paper.
This result also presents the endoscopic classification of unipotent representations of
G_{2}(F)
promised in the title of this paper, stated here only for tempered representations to simplify the exposition.
Let
\pi
be a tempered unipotent representation of
G_{2}(F)
with Langlands parameter
\phi
.
Every
s\in\mathcal{S}_{\psi}
determines an endoscopic triple
(G,s,\xi)
and a factorization
\phi=\xi\circ\phi^{s}
, for a Langlands parameter
\phi^{s}
for
G
.
In this case, Theorem
4.7
gives

(3)

\Theta_{\pi}=\sum_{(G,s,\xi)}(-1)^{\dim(\phi^{s})-\dim(\pi)}\frac{\overline{\langle s,\pi\rangle}}{{|Z_{A_{\phi}}(s)|}}\ \Lift_{(G,s,\xi)}\Theta^{G}_{\phi^{s}},

where the sum is taken over equivalence classes of endoscopic triples
(G,s,\xi)
with
s\in\mathcal{S}^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
and where we identify
s
with its image under
\mathcal{S}^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}\to A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
in the calculation of
Z_{A_{\phi}}(s)
.
This result is generalized from tempered unipotent representations to a wider class of unipotent representations in Theorem
4.7
.

0.5.
Next steps

Before we can declare that the ABV-packets of this paper are indeed generalized Arthur packets for
G_{2}(F)
, the following two issues must be addressed:

(i)
we must show that
\Theta^{G}_{\phi}
is a stable distribution for every unramified Langlands
\phi
of
G(F)
of Arthur type, where
G(F)
is an endoscopic group for
G_{2}(F)
; and

(ii)
we must show that, if
(G,s,\xi)
is an endoscopic triple for
G_{2}(F)
, then

\Theta^{G}_{\phi}(f^{s})=\Theta_{\xi\circ\phi,s}(f)

where
f^{s}\in\mathcal{H}(G(F))
is the Langlads-Shelstad transfer of
f\in\mathcal{H}(G_{2}(F))
; in other words, we must show that
\Theta_{\xi\circ\phi,s}
is the endoscopic lift of
\Theta^{G}_{\phi}
.
In fact, it is a conjecture of Vogan that
(i)
is true for all Langlands parameters, not just those of Arthur type. This is elementary for unramified parameters except when
G=G_{2}
and, as we show in Theorem
2.14
, in that case it is sufficient to show that
\Theta_{\phi}
is stable for the four elliptic, tempered, unipotent
L
-packets in Table
2.10.1
; see also Remark
2.16
. We are working on a proof of this statement.
We will also prove a generalization of
(ii)
that asks only that
\phi
is
s
-conormal, as in Definition
9
, again generalizing from the case of Langlands parameters of Arthur type.

0.6.
Geometry and arithmetic

ABV-packet coefficients are defined by a microlocal analysis of the moduli space of unramified Langlands parameters, following
[
9
]
, which builds on
[
28
]
.
As in
[
9
]
and
[
10
]
, we define the
infinitesimal parameter
of a Langlands parameter
\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G_{2}}}
is
\lambda_{\phi}:W_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G_{2}}}
to be
\lambda_{\phi}(w)=\phi(w,\diag(|w|^{1/2},|w|^{-1/2}))
.
There is a natural moduli space for Langlands parameters with the same infinitesimal parameter as
\phi
, introduced by Vogan and used extensively in
[
9
]
and
[
10
]
.
This moduli space is denoted by
V_{\lambda_{\phi}}
in this paper and it naturally carries an action of the reductive group
H_{\lambda_{\phi}}{\,:=\,}Z_{{\widehat{G_{2}}}}(\lambda_{\phi})
; it is, in particular, a prehomogeneous vector space.
In this paper we review the bijection between simple objects in the category
\mathsf{Per}_{H_{\lambda_{\phi}}}(V_{\lambda_{\phi}})
equivariant perverse sheaves on
V_{\lambda_{\phi}}
and irreducible admissible representations of
G_{2}(F)
for which the Langlands parameter has infinitesimal parameter
\lambda_{\phi}
.

We show that the Aubert involution of admissible representations appears as the Fourier transform of equivariant perverse sheaves on the other side of this bijection.
We also show how to recognize when a unipotent representation is tempered, Arthur type, unitary, generic, spherical or supercuspidal purely in terms of the geometry of the corresponding simple equivariant perverse sheaf.
To explain this, we say that
\phi
is
open
(resp.
closed
) if the
Z_{{\widehat{G_{2}}}}(\lambda_{\phi})
-orbit of
\phi
is open (resp. closed) in
V_{\lambda_{\phi}}
.
If
\pi
is tempered then its Langlands parameter is open but the converse is not true; in this sense, the notion of open parameters generalizes the notion of parameters bounded upon restriction to
W_{F}
.
We remark that a parameter
\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G_{2}}}
is closed if and only if it is trivial on the unipotent part of
\SL_{2}({\mathbb{C}})
appearing in
W^{\prime}_{F}{\,:=\,}W_{F}\times\SL_{2}({\mathbb{C}})
.
Returning to the claim that ABV-packet coefficients provide an extension of the Langlands correspondence and Arthur packets, we use these notions to prove fundamental properties of ABV-packet coefficients in Theorem
2.2
; for example, in Part
2
of Theorem
2.2
we show that if
\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G_{2}}}
is open or closed then
\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}(G_{2}(F))\to\widehat{A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}}
is bijective. In
[
CFZ
]
we found examples when
\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}(G_{2}(F))\to\widehat{A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}}
is not bijective.
In Part
4
of Theorem
2.2
we show that a representation
\pi\in\Pi_{\phi}(G_{2}(F))
is spherical if and only if
\phi
is closed and
\langle\ ,\pi\rangle
is the trivial representation of
A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
, and in this case, the only ABV-packet that contains
\pi
is
\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}(G_{2}(F))
.

0.7.
Notation

Throughout this paper
F
denotes a
F
-adic field,
\varphi
a fixed uniformizer in the ring
\mathcal{O}_{F}
of integers of
F
and
q
denotes the cardinality of the residue field of
F
; we place no lower bound on
q
.

We will use "polar notation"
\chi=\mu\nu^{a}
for complex characters
\chi
of
F^{\times}
, where
\nu:F^{\times}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
is the unramified character defined by
\nu(\varpi)=q
and where
\mu:F^{\times}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
is unitary and
a\in{\mathbb{R}}
. When subscripts are necessary, this notation becomes
\chi_{i}=\mu_{i}\nu^{a_{i}}
. We will use the notation
\theta_{n}
for a fixed, unitary character of
F^{\times}
of order
n
.

Similarly, we use the "
q
-polar notation"
z=uq^{a}
for complex numbers
z\in{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
where
u
is unitary and
a\in{\mathbb{R}}
.

By abuse of notation, we also denote a fixed primitive complex
n
-th root-of-unity by
\theta_{n}
and write
\vartheta_{n}
for the corresponding character of
\langle\theta_{n}\rangle{\,:=\,}\{1,\theta_{n},\ldots,\theta_{n}^{n-1}\}
, so
\vartheta_{n}(\theta_{n})=\theta_{n}
.
When the order
n
is understood by context, we will write
\vartheta
for this character.

We write
\varepsilon
for the sign character of
S_{3}
and
\varrho
for the reflection representation of
S_{3}
.

For a split group
G
over
F
with a fixed Borel
B
and torus
T
over
F
, we set

I^{G}(\sigma){\,:=\,}\text{Ind}_{B(F)}^{G(F)}(\sigma)
, where
\sigma
is a representation of
T(F)
, inflated to
B(F)
; we write
J^{G}(\sigma)
for the Langlands quotient
I^{G}(\sigma)
when this induced representation is a standard module.
More generally, if
P
is a parabolic subgroup of
G
with Levi subgroup
M
, we set

I_{P}^{G}(\sigma){\,:=\,}\text{Ind}_{P(F)}^{G(F)}(\sigma)
; we write
J_{P}^{G}(\sigma)
for the Langlands quotient of
I_{P}^{G}(\sigma)
when this induced representation is a standard module.

For an algebraic group
G
over
F
, denote by
1_{G}
the trivial representation of
G(F)
and
\mathrm{St}_{G}
the Steinberg representation of
G(F)
.

For representations of
G_{2}(F)
, we follow the notations from
[
25
]
except that the Steinberg representation of
\GL_{2}(F)
is denoted by
\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}}
instead of
\delta(1)
as in
[
25
]
.

There are 4 cuspidal unipotent representations of
G_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
: they appear as
G_{2}[1]
,
G_{2}[-1]
,
G_{2}[\theta_{3}]
and
G_{2}[\theta^{2}_{3}]
in
[
7
]
*p.460. For a cuspidal unipotent representation
\sigma
of
G_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
, we use the shorthand
I_{0}(\sigma)
for
\mathrm{cInd}_{G_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{F})}^{G_{2}(F)}(\sigma)
.
These supercuspidal representations also appear in
[
8
]
, where they are denoted by
v_{6}=I_{0}(G_{2}[1])
,
v_{7}=I_{0}(G_{2}[-1])
,
v_{8}=I_{0}(G_{2}[\theta_{3}])
and
v_{9}=I_{0}(G_{2}[\theta^{2}_{3}])
.
The last two of these also appear in
[
26
]
*Section 4, where they are denoted by
\pi^{\prime}[\nu]
and
\pi^{\prime}[\nu^{2}]
.

Throughout this paper, we use the notation
{\mathbbm{1}}_{X}
for the constant sheaf on
X
, or simply
{\mathbbm{1}}
if the support
X
is understood.

0.8.
Acknowledgements

The authors gratefully acknowledge and thank Goran Muić for explaining his work on admissible representations of
G_{2}(F)
and Maartin Solleveld for helpful comments on the first draft this paper.
The authors would also like to thank all the members of the
Voganish Project
, especially Ahmed Moussaoui, Jerrod Smith and Geoff Vooys.

1.
Geometry of the moduli space of Langlands parameters for
G_{2}

1.1.
Roots for
G_{2}
and
{\widehat{G_{2}}}

Let
T
be a fixed maximal split torus of
G_{2}
; the corresponding set of roots are denoted by
R{\,:=\,}R(G_{2},T)
. We write
W
for the Weyl group for this root system
R
.
Let
{\gamma_{1}},{\gamma_{2}}:T\rightarrow F^{\times}
be a choice simple roots of
G_{2}
with
{\gamma_{1}}
short and
{\gamma_{2}}
long so that the positive roots
R
are

{\gamma_{1}},{\gamma_{2}},{\gamma_{1}}+{\gamma_{2}},2{\gamma_{1}}+{\gamma_{2}},3{\gamma_{1}}+{\gamma_{2}},3{\gamma_{1}}+2{\gamma_{2}}.

The coroots are denoted
R^{\vee}{\,:=\,}R(G_{2},T)^{\vee}
, with
{\gamma_{1}^{\vee}},{\gamma_{2}^{\vee}}:F^{\times}\to T
the coroots of
{\gamma_{1}}
and
{\gamma_{2}}
.
We fix the isomorphism
T\rightarrow F^{\times}\times F^{\times}
by

t\mapsto((2{\gamma_{1}}+{\gamma_{2}})(t),({\gamma_{1}}+{\gamma_{2}})(t)).

We use the notation
m:F^{\times}\times F^{\times}\to T
for the inverse of this isomorphism.
We have

{\gamma_{1}^{\vee}}(a)=m(a,a^{-1}),\qquad\text{\ and\ }\qquad{\gamma_{2}^{\vee}}(a)=m(1,a).

We denote by
{\widehat{G_{2}}}
the dual group of
G_{2}
over
\mathbb{C}
and
{\widehat{T}}
the dual torus and let
{\hat{R}}{\,:=\,}R({\widehat{G_{2}}},{\widehat{T}})
be the roots of
{\widehat{G_{2}}}
with the usual identification of coroots of
G_{2}
with roots of
{\widehat{G_{2}}}
. We again write
W
for the Weyl group for the root system
{\hat{R}}
.
Denote by
{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}\in{\hat{R}}
(resp.
{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}\in{\hat{R}}
) the image of
{\gamma_{1}^{\vee}}
(resp.
{\gamma_{2}^{\vee}}
) under this identification.
Then
\widehat{G}_{2}
is a complex reductive group of type
G_{2}
, with simple roots
{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}},{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}
.
where
{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}
is a long root of
\widehat{G}_{2}
and
{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}
is a short root of
\widehat{G}_{2}
.
As above, we fix an isomorphism
{\widehat{T}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
by

t\mapsto(({\hat{\gamma}_{1}}+2{\hat{\gamma}_{2}})(t),({\hat{\gamma}_{1}}+{\hat{\gamma}_{2}})(t))

and we write
\widehat{m}:\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}\to{\widehat{T}}
for the inverse of this isomorphism.
Note that we have

(4)

{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}(\widehat{m}(x,y))=x^{-1}y^{2},\qquad\mathrm{\ and\ }\qquad{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}(\widehat{m}(x,y))=xy^{-1}.

We will denote by
{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}^{\vee},{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}^{\vee}:\mathbb{C}^{\times}\rightarrow{\widehat{T}}
the coroots of
{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}
and
{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}
.
We have again that

{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}^{\vee}(a)={\widehat{m}}(1,a),\quad{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}^{\vee}(a)={\widehat{m}}(a,a^{-1}).

Observe that under the identification
R(G_{2},T)=R({\widehat{G_{2}}},{\widehat{T}})^{\vee}
, we have

{\gamma_{1}}={{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}^{\vee}
,

{\gamma_{2}}={{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}^{\vee}
,

({{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}})^{\vee}=3{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}^{\vee}+{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}^{\vee}
,

({{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}})^{\vee}=3{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}^{\vee}+2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}^{\vee}
,

({{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+3{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}})^{\vee}={{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}^{\vee}+{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}^{\vee}
and

(2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+3{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}})^{\vee}=2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}^{\vee}+{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}^{\vee}
.

1.2.
Moduli spaces of Langlands parameters

Set
W^{\prime}_{F}{\,:=\,}W_{F}\times\SL_{2}({\mathbb{C}})
.
Given a Langlands parameter
\phi:W_{F}^{\prime}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G_{2}}}
, recall that its
infinitesimal parameter

\lambda_{\phi}:W_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G_{2}}}
is defined by
\lambda_{\phi}(w)=\phi(w,\diag(|w|^{1/2},|w|^{-1/2}))
.

For a given infinitesimal parameter
\lambda:W_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G_{2}}}
, there are only a finite number of
{\widehat{G_{2}}}
-conjugacy classes of Langlands parameters
\phi
with
\lambda=\lambda_{\phi}
.
As explained in
[
9
]
more generally, each unramified infinitesimal parameter
\lambda:W_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G_{2}}}
determines a prehomogeneous vector space

V_{\lambda}=\{x\in\operatorname{Lie}{\widehat{G_{2}}}{\ |\ }\Ad(\lambda(w))x=q^{|w|}x,\forall w\in W_{F}\},

equipped with an action of the group

H_{\lambda}{\,:=\,}\{g\in{\widehat{G_{2}}}{\ |\ }\lambda(w)g\lambda(w)^{-1}=g,\forall w\in W_{F}\}.

acting via the adjoint action of
{\widehat{G_{2}}}
on
\operatorname{Lie}{\widehat{G_{2}}}
.
Using this action we can assume that
\lambda({\operatorname{Fr}})\in{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{T}}
and define

{\hat{R}}_{\lambda}{\,:=\,}\{{\hat{\gamma}}\in R({\widehat{G}},{\widehat{T}}){\ |\ }\operatorname{Lie}U_{\hat{\gamma}}\subseteq V_{\lambda}\},

where
U_{\hat{\gamma}}\subset{\widehat{G_{2}}}
is the root subgroup for
{\hat{\gamma}}
.
Then
V_{\lambda}
is a moduli space for all Langlands parameters
\phi
with
\lambda_{\phi}=\lambda
and
X_{\lambda}{\,:=\,}{\widehat{G_{2}}}\times_{H_{\lambda}}V_{\lambda}
is a moduli space for all Langlands parameters
\phi
for which
\lambda_{\phi}
is
{\widehat{G_{2}}}
-conjugate to
\lambda
. Note that
{\widehat{G_{2}}}
acts naturally on
X_{\lambda}
while
H_{\lambda}
acts on
V_{\lambda}
.

These notions lead to the following classification of unramified infinitesimal parameters
\lambda
for
G_{2}(F)
.

Proposition 1.1
.

For every unramified
\lambda:W_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G_{2}}}
, the set
{\hat{R}}_{\lambda}
is
W
-conjugate to one of the following subsets of
{\hat{R}}
, also pictured in Table
1.2.1
:

(1)

{\hat{R}}_{\lambda}=\emptyset
;

(2)

{\hat{R}}_{\lambda}=\{\hat{\gamma}_{1}+2\hat{\gamma}_{2}\}
;

(3)

{\hat{R}}_{\lambda}=\{2\hat{\gamma}_{1}+3\hat{\gamma}_{2}\}
;

(4)

{\hat{R}}_{\lambda}=\{\hat{\gamma}_{1},2\hat{\gamma}_{1}+3\hat{\gamma}_{2}\}
;

(5)

{\hat{R}}_{\lambda}=\{\hat{\gamma}_{1},\hat{\gamma}_{1}+2\hat{\gamma}_{2}\}
;

(6)

{\hat{R}}_{\lambda}=\{\hat{\gamma}_{2},\hat{\gamma}_{1}+\hat{\gamma}_{2}\}
;

(7)

{\hat{R}}_{\lambda}=\{\hat{\gamma}_{1},\hat{\gamma}_{1}+3\hat{\gamma}_{2}\}
;

(8)

{\hat{R}}_{\lambda}=\{\hat{\gamma}_{1},\hat{\gamma}_{2}\}
;

(9)

{\hat{R}}_{\lambda}=\{\hat{\gamma}_{1},\hat{\gamma}_{1}+\hat{\gamma}_{2},\hat{\gamma}_{1}+2\hat{\gamma}_{2},\hat{\gamma}_{1}+3\hat{\gamma}_{2}\}
.

Each case above determines a moduli space
V_{\lambda}
of unramified Langlands parameters with infinitesimal parameter
\lambda
.

Table 1.2.1.
The classification of unramified infinitesimal parameters
\lambda
for
G_{2}(F)
in terms of the subsets
{\hat{R}}_{\lambda}
appearing in Proposition
1.1
. Black root vectors with a halo appear in
{\hat{R}}_{\lambda}
and so the corresponding root spaces form
V_{\lambda}
. Vectors in black without a halo determine root groups appearing in the reductive group
H_{\lambda}
acting on
V_{\lambda}

Proof.

The possibilities for
\lambda
are classified by the associated prehomogeneous vector spaces
V_{\lambda}
as they appear in
\operatorname{Lie}{\widehat{G_{2}}}
up to
{\widehat{G}}_{2}
-conjugacy.

Case
1
describes all
\lambda
for which
V_{\lambda}=0
.
These are the
\lambda
for which
{\hat{\gamma}}(\lambda({\operatorname{Fr}}))\neq q
for every root
{\hat{\gamma}}\in{\hat{R}}
.
In this case the group
H_{\lambda}
is
{\widehat{G_{2}}}
or one of the subgroups
\SL_{3}(\mathbb{C})
,
\SO_{4}(\mathbb{C})
,
\GL_{2}^{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}({\mathbb{C}})
,
\GL_{2}^{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}({\mathbb{C}})
or
{\widehat{T}}
, where
U_{\hat{\gamma}}\subset\GL_{2}^{\hat{\gamma}}
.

Cases
2
and
3
describe all
\lambda
for which
\dim V_{\lambda}=1
; in these cases,
V_{\lambda}=\mathrm{Span}\{X_{\hat{\gamma}}\}
for some root
{\hat{\gamma}}
of
{\widehat{G}}_{2}
and
H_{\lambda}
is either
{\widehat{T}}
or
\GL_{2}^{\hat{\gamma}^{\prime}}({\mathbb{C}})
for a root
{\hat{\gamma}^{\prime}}
perpendicular to
{\hat{\gamma}}
.
Up to
{\widehat{G}}_{2}
-conjugacy, there are only two cases: either
{\hat{\gamma}}
is short or long; in Case
2
we consider the short root
{\hat{\gamma}}={{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}
, to which
{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}
is perpendicular, and in Case
3
we consider the long root
{\hat{\gamma}}=2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+3{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}
, to which
{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}
is perpendicular.

Cases
4
,
5
,
6
,
7
and
8
describe all
\lambda
for which
\dim V_{\lambda}=2
; in these cases,
V_{\lambda}=\mathrm{Span}\{X_{\widehat{\gamma}},X_{\widehat{\gamma}^{\prime}}\}
for roots
{\widehat{\gamma}}
and
X_{\hat{\gamma}^{\prime}}
of
{\widehat{G}}_{2}
and
H_{\lambda}
is either
{\widehat{T}}
or
\GL_{2}^{\hat{\gamma}_{0}}
for a root
{\hat{\gamma}_{0}}
such that
{\widehat{\gamma}^{\prime}}={\widehat{\gamma}}+{\widehat{\gamma}_{0}}
.

If
\dim V_{\lambda}>2
there is only one possibility for
\lambda
and this is Case
9
, treated in
[
10
]
.
When
\dim V_{\lambda}>2
this forces
\dim V_{\lambda}=4
and that

V_{\lambda}=\mathrm{Span}\{X_{\widehat{\gamma}},X_{{\widehat{\gamma}}+{\widehat{\gamma}_{0}}},X_{{\widehat{\gamma}}+2{\widehat{\gamma}_{0}}},X_{{\widehat{\gamma}}+3{\widehat{\gamma}_{0}}}\}

for roots
\hat{\gamma}
and
{\widehat{\gamma}_{0}}
.
The group that acts on
V_{\lambda}
in this case is
H_{\lambda}=\GL_{2}^{\widehat{\gamma}_{0}}
.
There are exactly six such
V_{\lambda}
, all
{\widehat{G}}_{2}
-conjugate to
\mathrm{Span}\{X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}},X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}},X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}},X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+3{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}}\}
, in which case
H_{\lambda}=\GL_{2}^{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}}
.
∎

1.3.
Prehomogeneous vector spaces

This classification of infinitesimal parameters
\lambda
for
G_{2}
by the associated prehomogeneous vector spaces
H_{\lambda}\times V_{\lambda}\to V_{\lambda}
given in Proposition
1.1
may be simplified further by examining the categories
\mathsf{Per}_{H_{\lambda}}(V_{\lambda})
that arise as
\lambda
goes through the cases appearing there.

Proposition 1.2
.

If
\lambda:W_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G_{2}}}
is an unramified infinitesimal parameter for
G_{2}(F)
then the category
\mathsf{Per}_{H_{\lambda}}(V_{\lambda})
is equivalent to
\mathsf{Per}_{H}(V)
where
H\times V\to V
is one of the following five prehomogeneous vector spaces.

(1)

V=0
and
H
is an algebraic group, not necessarily connected (trivial action);

(2)

V=\mathbb{A}^{1}
with
H=\GL_{1}
-action given by
t\cdot x=tx
(scalar multiplication);

(3)

V=\mathbb{A}^{2}
with
H=\GL_{2}
-action
h\cdot x=det(h)^{n}hx
, for non-negative integer
n
(twisted matrix multiplication);

(4)

V=\mathbb{A}^{2}
with
H=\GL_{1}^{2}
-action
(t_{1},t_{2})\cdot(x_{1},x_{2})=(t_{1}x_{1},t_{1}t_{2}^{n}x_{2})
, for positive integer
n
(toric variety);

(5)

V=\mathbb{A}^{4}
with
H=\GL_{2}
-action
h\cdot x=(\det^{-1}\otimes\Sym^{3})(h)(x)=\det(h)^{-1}\Sym^{3}(h)x
(normalized Symmetric cube).

Proof.

We begin the proof with a general observation.
Suppose
H
acts on
V
and let
u:H^{\prime}\to H
be an epimorphism of algebraic groups with connected kernel.
Let
H^{\prime}
act on
V
through
u
. Then
\mathsf{Per}_{H}(V)
and
\mathsf{Per}_{H^{\prime}}(V)
are equivalent.
To see this, recall that an
H
-equivariant perverse sheaf on
V
is a perverse sheaf
\mathcal{F}
equipped with an isomorphism
\mu:m^{*}\mathcal{F}\to p^{*}\mathcal{F}
satisfying a so-called cocycle condition.
Consider the functor
\mathsf{Per}_{H}(V)\to\mathsf{Per}_{H^{\prime}}(V)
defined on objects by
(\mathcal{F},\mu)\mapsto(\mathcal{F},(u\times\id)^{*}\mu)
and on arrows by
\varphi\mapsto\varphi
.
This functor is essentially surjective.
Since
u\times\id:H^{\prime}\times V\to H\times V
is smooth with connected fibres of equal dimension
\dim\ker u
the functor
(u\times\id)^{*}
is full and faithful by, for example,
[
4
]
*Proposition 4.2.5. Thus,
\mathsf{Per}_{H}(V)
and
\mathsf{Per}_{H^{\prime}}(V)
are equivalent.
We refer to this equivalence as an instance of base-change, below, and in this case we will say that the prehomogeneous vector spaces
H\times V\to V
and
H^{\prime}\times V\to V
are equivalent.

The cases below all refer to Proposition
1.1
.

(Case
1
)

The prehomogeneous vector space in Proposition
1.1
, Case
1
is
V=0
for a connected group acting on
0
, which is an instance of the prehomogeneous vector space
1
. By the base-change argument above, this is equivalent to
1
for trivial group
H
.

(Case
2
)

There are two group actions appearing in Proposition
1.1
, Case
2
, either
H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:1-short}}}={\widehat{T}}
or
H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:1-short}}}=\GL_{2}
.
In the former case, observe that
{\hat{\gamma}_{1}}+2{\hat{\gamma}_{2}}:{\widehat{T}}\to{\GL_{1}}
is an epimorphism of algebraic groups with connected fibre so, by the paragraph above,
\mathsf{Per}_{H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:1-short}}}}(V_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:1-short}}})
is equivalent to
\mathsf{Per}_{{\GL_{1}}}(\mathbb{A}^{1})
for the prehomogeneous vector space
2
.
In the latter case, observe that
\det:{\widehat{T}}\to{\GL_{1}}
is an epimorphism of algebraic groups with connected fibre so, by the base-change argument above
\mathsf{Per}_{H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:1-short}}}}(V_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:1-short}}})
is again equivalent to
\mathsf{Per}_{{\GL_{1}}}(\mathbb{A}^{1})
for the prehomogeneous vector space
2
.

(Case
3
)

In Proposition
1.1
, Case
3
there are two group actions to consider: either
H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:2-long}}}={\widehat{T}}
or
H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:2-long}}}=\GL_{2}
.
Here,
V_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:2-long}}}=\mathbb{A}^{1}
.
If
H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:2-long}}}={\widehat{T}}
then the action is
h.x=\det(h)x
.
Using the base-change argument above we have
\mathsf{Per}_{H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:2-long}}}}(V_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:2-long}}})=\mathsf{Per}_{{\GL_{1}}}(\mathbb{A}^{1})
since
2{\hat{\gamma}_{1}}+3{\hat{\gamma}_{2}}:{\widehat{T}}\to{\GL_{1}}
is an epimorphism of algebraic groups with connected fibre. If
H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:2-long}}}=\GL_{2}
then the action is
h\cdot x=\det(h)x
.
Then, using the base-change argument above,
\mathsf{Per}_{H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:2-long}}}}(V_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:2-long}}})=\mathsf{Per}_{{\GL_{1}}}(\mathbb{A}^{1})
since
\det:\GL_{2}\to{\GL_{1}}
is an epimorphism of algebraic groups with connected fibre.
Thus,
\mathsf{Per}_{H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:2-long}}}}(V_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:2-long}}})
is equivalent
\mathsf{Per}_{H}(V)
for the prehomogeneous vector space
2
.

(Case
4
)

The prehomogeneous vector space in Proposition
1.1
, Case
4
is
V_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:3}}}=\mathbb{A}^{2}
and
H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:3}}}=\GL_{2}
; this is equivalent to the prehomogeneous vector space
3
for
n=1
.

(Case
5
)

The prehomogeneous vector space in Proposition
1.1
, Case
5
is
V_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:4-D2}}}=\mathbb{A}^{2}
and
H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:4-D2}}}={\widehat{T}}
; this is equivalent to the prehomogeneous vector space
4
for
n=2
.

(Case
6
)

The prehomogeneous vector space in Proposition
1.1
, Case
6
is
V_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:5}}}=\mathbb{A}^{2}
and
H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:5}}}=\GL_{2}
; this is equivalent to the prehomogeneous vector space
3
for
n=0
.

(Case
7
)

The prehomogeneous vector space in Proposition
1.1
, Case
7
is
V_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:6-A2}}}=\mathbb{A}^{2}
and
H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:6-A2}}}={\widehat{T}}
; this is equivalent to the prehomogeneous vector space
4
for
n=3
.

(Case
8
)

The prehomogeneous vector space in Proposition
1.1
, Case
8
is
V_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:7-reg}}}=\mathbb{A}^{2}
and
H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:7-reg}}}={\widehat{T}}
; this is equivalent to the prehomogeneous vector space
4
for
n=1
.

(Case
9
)

The prehomogeneous vector space in Proposition
1.1
, Case
9
is
V_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:8-sub}}}=\mathbb{A}^{4}
and
H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:8-sub}}}=\GL_{2}
; as explained in
[
10
]
*Proposition 3.1, this is equivalent to the prehomogeneous vector space
5
.
Category
\mathsf{Per}_{\GL_{2}}(\det^{-1}\otimes\Sym^{3})
appeared in
[
10
]
.∎

1.4.
The geometry of the moduli space of Langlands parameters

In this section we make a microlocal study of the five prehomogeneous vector spaces appearing in Proposition
1.2
. In Section
2.6
we use this to determine the ABV-packets for
G_{2}(F)
.

Let
H\times V\to V
be a prehomogeneous vector space.
Let
V^{*}
be the dual vector space to
V
and let
H\times V^{*}\to V^{*}
be the adjoint action. Then
H\times H
acts on
T^{*}(V)=V\times V^{*}
by
(h,k)\cdot(x,y){\,:=\,}(h\cdot x,k\cdot y)
and
T^{*}(V)
is also a prehomogeneous vector space.
Now let
[\,\ ]:V\times V^{*}\to\operatorname{Lie}H
be the momentum map and consider the conormal variety

\Lambda{\,:=\,}\{(x,y)\in T^{*}(V),\ [x,y]=0\}.

This Lagrangian subspace
\Lambda\subseteq T^{*}(V)
carries the diagonal
H
-action
h\cdot(x,y){\,:=\,}(h\cdot x,h\cdot y)
.
This is a bundle of vector spaces with group actions: for each
x\in V
, the fibre

\Lambda_{x}=\{y\in V^{*},\ [x,y]=0\}

caries a natural action of
Z_{H}(x)
.
It is not always the case that
\Lambda_{x}
is a prehomogeneous vector space.

Proposition 1.3
.

If
V
is one of the 5 prehomogeneous vector spaces appearing in Proposition
1.2
then
\Lambda
is a bundle of prehomogeneous vector spaces.

When the conormal variety
\Lambda
to
V
is a bundle of prehomogeneous vector spaces, we write
\Lambda_{x}^{\text{sreg}}
for the open dense
Z_{H}(x)
-orbit in
\Lambda_{x}
and
\Lambda^{\text{reg}}_{C}
for the open dense
H
-orbit in
\Lambda_{C}
, for each
H
-orbit
C\subseteq V
.

In this section we study the conormal varieties to the prehomogeneous vector spaces appearing in Proposition
1.2
. In each case we:

•

find all
H
-orbits
C\subseteq V
and all dual
H
-orbits
C^{*}\subseteq V^{*}
;

•

calculate each equivariant fundamental group
A_{C}{\,:=\,}\pi_{0}(Z_{H}(x))
, for
x\in C
;

•

enumerate the simple objects in
\mathsf{Per}_{H}(V)
;

•

find the Fourier transform of each simple equivariant perverse sheaf on
V
;

•

show that the conormal variety
\Lambda
is a bundle of prehomogeneous vector spaces;

•

calculate the equivariant fundamental group
A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{C}
of
\Lambda_{C}^{\text{sreg}}
, for each
H
-orbit
C
in
V
;

•

calculate
\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}_{C_{j}}{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}(\mathcal{L}_{C_{i}})\in\mathsf{Loc}_{H}(\Lambda_{C_{j}}^{\text{sreg}})
for every simple
{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}(\mathcal{L}_{C_{i}})
in
\mathsf{Per}_{H}(V)
, where
\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}
is defined in
[
9
]
*Section 7.10.

These geometric calculations, and their applications to representation theory, follow the ideas presented in the examples treated in
[
9
]
*Part II and in
[
10
]
.

(
1
)

\mathsf{Per}_{H}(0)
is the category of finite dimensional representations of
\pi_{0}(H)
.
Then
\Lambda=0
and the functor
\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}
is the identity.

(
2
)

For the action of
\GL_{1}
on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
given by scalar multiplication there are two
{\GL_{1}}
-orbits:
C_{-}=\{0\}
and its complement,
C_{1}
.
Then
\mathsf{Per}_{{\GL_{1}}}(\mathbb{A}^{1})
has two simple objects,
{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{0}})={\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{0}}^{!}[0]
, the extension-by-zero of
{\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{0}}
and
{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{1}})={\mathbbm{1}}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}[1]
.
The conormal varieties are given as follows.
First observe that
\Lambda_{0}=T_{0}(\mathbb{A}^{1}){\ \cong\ }\mathbb{A}^{1}
and
\Lambda_{0}^{\text{sreg}}{\ \cong\ }\{y\in\mathbb{A}^{1}{\ |\ }y\neq 0\}
; note that this is a single orbit under the
\GL_{1}
-action.
If
x\in C_{1}
then
\Lambda_{x}=\{(x,y){\ |\ }xy=0\}=\{(x,0)\}
which itself is a
\GL_{1}
-orbit.
In this way we see that
T^{*}_{C_{0}}(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\text{sreg}}
and
T^{*}_{C_{1}}(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\text{sreg}}
are both isomorphic, as
\GL_{1}
-spaces, to
C_{1}
and consequently, equivariant local systems on these spaces are naturally identified with finite-dimensional vector spaces.
The functor
\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}:\mathsf{Per}_{\GL_{1}}(\mathbb{A}^{1})\to\mathsf{Loc}_{\GL_{1}}(T^{*}_{\GL_{1}}(\mathbb{A}^{1})_{\text{sreg}})
is given by the following table:

\begin{array}[]{c| cc | c}\mathsf{Per}_{\GL_{1}}(\mathbb{A}^{1})&\mathsf{Loc}_{\GL_{1}}(\Lambda_{C_{0}}^{\text{sreg}})&\mathsf{Loc}_{\GL_{1}}(\Lambda_{C_{1}}^{\text{sreg}})&\text{Fourier}\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{0}})&{\mathbbm{1}}_{\Lambda_{C_{0}}^{\text{sreg}}}&0&{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{1}})\\
{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{1}})&0&{\mathbbm{1}}_{\Lambda_{C_{1}}^{\text{sreg}}}&{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{0}})\end{array}

(
3
)

For the twisted matrix multiplication action of
\GL_{2}
on
\mathbb{A}^{2}
, again there are only two
H
-orbits,
C_{0}=\{(0,0)\}
and the complement
C_{1}
and only two simple objects in
\mathsf{Per}_{\GL_{2}}(\mathbb{A}^{2})
are
{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{0}})={\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{0}}^{!}[0]
and
{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{1}})={\mathbbm{1}}_{\mathbb{A}^{2}}[2]
.
Again,
T^{*}_{C_{0}}(\mathbb{A}^{2})^{\text{sreg}}
and
T^{*}_{C_{1}}(\mathbb{A}^{2})^{\text{sreg}}
are both isomorphic, as
\GL_{2}
-spaces, to
{\bar{C}_{1}}
, which is a single
\GL_{2}
-orbit and also has trivial equivariant fundamental group.
The functor
\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}:\mathsf{Per}_{\GL_{2}}(\mathbb{A}^{2})\to\mathsf{Loc}_{\GL_{2}}(T^{*}_{\GL_{2}}(\mathbb{A}^{2})_{\text{sreg}})
is given by

\begin{array}[]{c| cc | c }\mathsf{Per}_{\GL_{2}}(\mathbb{A}^{2})&\mathsf{Loc}_{\GL_{2}}(\Lambda_{C_{0}}^{\text{sreg}})&\mathsf{Loc}_{\GL_{2}}(\Lambda_{C_{1}}^{\text{sreg}})&\text{Fourier}\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{0}})&{\mathbbm{1}}_{\Lambda_{C_{0}}^{\text{sreg}}}&0&{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{1}})\\
{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{1}})&0&{\mathbbm{1}}_{\Lambda_{C_{1}}^{\text{sreg}}}&{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{0}})\end{array}

(
4
)

Consider the action of
H=\GL_{1}^{2}
on
V=\mathbb{A}^{2}
by
(t_{1},t_{2}).(x_{1},x_{2})=(t_{1}x_{1},t_{1}t_{2}^{n}x_{2})
for positive integer
n
.
There are four
H
-orbits in this case:
C_{0}=\{(0,0)\}
,
C_{1}=\{(x_{1},0){\ |\ }x_{1}\neq 0\}
,
C_{2}=\{(0,x_{2}){\ |\ }x_{2}\neq 0\}
and the open orbit
C_{3}=\{(x_{1},x_{2}){\ |\ }x_{1}x_{2}\neq 0\}
.
The equivariant fundamental groups of these orbits are trivial with the exception of
C_{3}
, for which
A_{C_{3}}
is the group of
n
-th roots-of-unity.
There are
4+(n-1)
simple objects in this category:
{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{0}})={\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{0}}^{!}
,
{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{1}})={\mathbbm{1}}_{\bar{C}_{1}}^{!}[1]
,
{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{2}})={\mathbbm{1}}_{\bar{C}_{2}}^{!}[1]
,
{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{3}})={\mathbbm{1}}_{\mathbb{A}^{2}}[2]
and
{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}(\vartheta_{C_{3}})
, where
\vartheta
is a non-trivial character of the group of
n
th roots-of-unity.
For
x\in C_{0}
or
C_{3}
we have
\Lambda_{x}{\ \cong\ }{\bar{C}_{3}}
which has a unique open
\GL_{1}^{2}
-orbit.
For
x\in C_{1}
,
\Lambda_{x}=\{((x_{1},0),(y_{1},y_{2}))\in T^{*}(V){\ |\ }x_{1}y_{1}=0\}=\{((x_{1},0),(0,y_{2})){\ |\ }y_{2}\}{\ \cong\ }{\bar{C}_{2}}
which also has a unique
\GL_{1}^{2}
-orbit; likewise for
x\in C_{2}
,
\Lambda_{x}{\ \cong\ }{\bar{C}_{1}}
.
It follows that
\Lambda_{C}
has a unique open
H
-orbit,
\Lambda_{C}^{\text{sreg}}
, for every
H
-orbit
C\subset V
.
A simple calculation shows that the equivariant fundamental group of
\Lambda_{C}^{\text{sreg}}
is the group
\langle\theta_{n}\rangle
of
n
th roots-of-unity, for every
H
-orbit
C\subset V
.
The functor
\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}
is given by the following table, where the last row is removed if
n=1
and duplicated if
n>2
for every non-trivial character
\vartheta
.

\begin{array}[]{c | cccc | c}\mathsf{Per}_{\GL_{1}^{2}}(\mathbb{A}^{2})&\mathsf{Loc}_{\GL_{1}^{2}}(\Lambda_{C_{0}}^{\text{sreg}})&\mathsf{Loc}_{\GL_{1}^{2}}(\Lambda_{C_{1}}^{\text{sreg}})&\mathsf{Loc}_{\GL_{1}^{2}}(\Lambda_{C_{2}}^{\text{sreg}})&\mathsf{Loc}_{\GL_{1}^{2}}(\Lambda_{C_{3}}^{\text{sreg}})&\text{Fourier }\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{0}})&{\mathbbm{1}}_{\Lambda_{C_{0}}^{\text{sreg}}}&0&0&0&{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{3}})\\
{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{1}})&0&{\mathbbm{1}}_{\Lambda_{C_{1}}^{\text{sreg}}}&0&0&{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{2}})\\
{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{2}})&0&0&{\mathbbm{1}}_{\Lambda_{C_{2}}^{\text{sreg}}}&0&{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{1}})\\
{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{3}})&0&0&0&{\mathbbm{1}}_{\Lambda_{C_{3}}^{\text{sreg}}}&{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{0}})\\
\hline\cr{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}(\vartheta_{C_{3}})&\vartheta_{\Lambda_{0}^{\text{sreg}}}&\vartheta_{\Lambda_{1}^{\text{sreg}}}&\vartheta_{\Lambda_{1}^{\text{sreg}}}&\vartheta_{\Lambda_{2}^{\text{sreg}}}&{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}(\vartheta_{C_{3}})\end{array}

(
5
)

The functor
\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}
for
H=\GL_{2}
acting on
V=\mathbb{A}^{4}
by
h.x=\det^{-1}(h)\Sym^{3}(h)(x)
(nomalized Symmetric cube) was calculated in
[
10
]
. We recall the result in the table below, in which
\vartheta{\,:=\,}\vartheta_{2}
is the sign character of
\langle\theta_{2}\rangle
and, as explained in Section
0.7
,
\varepsilon
is the sign character of
S_{3}
and
\varrho
is the character of the reflection representation of
S_{3}
.

\begin{array}[]{ c | cccc | c }\mathsf{Per}_{\GL_{2}}(V)&\mathsf{Loc}_{\GL_{2}}(\Lambda_{C_{0}}^{\mathrm{sreg}})&\mathsf{Loc}_{\GL_{2}}(\Lambda_{C_{1}}^{\mathrm{sreg}})&\mathsf{Loc}_{\GL_{2}}(\Lambda_{C_{2}}^{\mathrm{sreg}})&\mathsf{Loc}_{\GL_{2}}(\Lambda_{C_{3}}^{\mathrm{sreg}})&\text{Fourier}\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{0}})&{\mathbbm{1}}_{\Lambda_{0}^{\mathrm{reg}}}&0&0&0&{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{3}})\\
{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{1}})&\varrho_{\Lambda_{0}^{\mathrm{reg}}}&{\mathbbm{1}}_{\Lambda_{1}^{\mathrm{reg}}}&0&0&{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}(\varrho_{C_{3}})\\
{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{2}})&0&\vartheta_{\Lambda_{1}^{\mathrm{reg}}}&{\mathbbm{1}}_{\Lambda_{2}^{\mathrm{reg}}}&0&{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{2}})\\
{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{3}})&0&0&0&{\mathbbm{1}}_{\Lambda_{3}^{\mathrm{reg}}}&{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{1}})\\
{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}(\varrho_{C_{3}})&0&0&\vartheta_{\Lambda_{2}^{\mathrm{reg}}}&\varrho_{\Lambda_{3}^{\mathrm{reg}}}&{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{0}})\\
{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}(\varepsilon_{C_{3}})&\varepsilon_{\Lambda_{0}^{\mathrm{reg}}}&{\mathbbm{1}}_{\Lambda_{1}^{\mathrm{reg}}}&\vartheta_{\Lambda_{2}^{\mathrm{reg}}}&\varepsilon_{\Lambda_{3}^{\mathrm{reg}}}&{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}(\varepsilon_{C_{3}})\end{array}

2.
Unipotent representations for
G_{2}

In this section we use the classification of infinitesimal parameters for
G_{2}(F)
in Proposition
1.1
, together with the results of Section
1.4
, to enumerate all irreducible unipotent representations of
G_{2}(F)
and to find their Langlands parameters. In this way we give an explicit form of the Langlands correspondence for unipotent representations of
G_{2}(F)
. At the same time, we will find the ABV-packet coefficients and start assembling the proof of Theorem
2.2
.

2.1.
The Langlands correspondence

The Langlands correspondence for unipotent representations of
G_{2}(F)
is a special case of
[
23
]
, though that paper uses a normalization of the correspondence that is different from the one we give here.
Instead, here we use a normalization of the local Langlands correspondence that satisfies the desiderata of
[
12
]
*§9; in particular, it satisfies
[
18
]
*Conjecture 2.6, which specifies the Langlands parameter of generic representations for a particular infinitesimal parameter.
Moreover, for a choice of hyperspecial group, our normalization also specifies the enhanced Langlands parameter of spherical representation for a particular infinitesimal parameter,
i.e.
, if
\pi\in\Pi_{\phi}(G_{2}(F))
is spherical, then
\phi
is trivial on the
\SL_{2}(\mathbb{C})
part and the corresponding representation of
\widehat{A}_{\phi}
is trivial.
This choice of normalization is compatible with the requirement in the work of Arthur
[
3
]
and agrees with the local Langlands correspondence appearing in
[
27
]
.
As in
[
29
]
*p. 804, this normalization is obtained by composing Lusztig’s correspondence with the Aubert involution.

The Langlands correspondence for unipotent representations of
G_{2}(F)
is given in Table
2.1.1
, also in Table
2.7.1
, making reference to the classification of unramified infinitesimal parameters in Proposition
1.1
and the enumeration of unramified Langlands parameters for
G_{2}(F)
given in Section
2.6
.
Both tables use notation from
[
25
]
for irreducible admissible representations of
G_{2}(F)
, modified as explained in Section
0.7
.
In Table
2.1.1
, characters of
A_{\phi}
are listed in the column with
\phi
at the top and the
L
-packet
\Pi_{\phi}(G_{2}(F))
is found by gathering together the representations
\pi
for which the character of
A_{\phi}
is non-zero, thus defining the bijection

\Pi_{\phi}(G_{2}(F))\to\widehat{A_{\phi}},

for all unramified Langlands parameters for
G_{2}(F)
.
In this way, Table
2.1.1
defines
\pi(\phi,\rho)
for each enhanced Langlands parameter
(\phi,\rho)
, for the
\rho\in\widehat{A_{\phi}}
.

There are precisely three non-singleton
L
-packets of unipotent representations of
G_{2}(F)
:
\Pi_{\phi_{\ref{infcase:4-D2}d}}(G_{2}(F))
,
\Pi_{\phi_{\ref{infcase:6-A2}d}}(G_{2}(F))
and
\Pi_{\phi_{\ref{infcase:8-sub}d}}(G_{2}(F))
, of which the first has order
2
and the other two have order
3
.
Note that the group
A_{\phi}
is trivial except when
\phi
is
\phi_{\ref{infcase:4-D2}d}
,
\phi_{\ref{infcase:6-A2}d}
or
\phi_{\ref{infcase:8-sub}d}
, in which cases
A_{\phi}
is
\langle\theta_{2}\rangle
,
\langle\theta_{3}\rangle
and
S_{3}
, respectively.
These three Langlands parameters are all of Arthur type and are distinguished by the fact that they have elliptic endoscopy, as explained in Section
4
.

Table 2.1.1.
The local Langlands correspondence for unipotent representations of
G_{2}(F)
. See Section
2.1
for how to read this table.

\hline\cr 1_{G_{2}}&&&&&&&&&&&&&&&&&&1&0&0&0&&&&\\

\pi(1)^{\prime}&&&&&&&&&&&&&&&&&&&&&&0&0&0&1\\

Recall that we have classified all unramified infinitesimal parameters for
G_{2}(F)
in Section
1.2
. Working through the 9 cases in Proposition
1.1
, in Section
2.6
we find, in each case, all Langlands parameters
\phi
with given infinitesimal parameter
\lambda
. Using results from
[
9
]
, we compute the component group
A_{\phi}
. We find that these groups are trivial in all cases except the unique tempered Langlands parameter with infinitesimal parameters given by Cases
5
,
7
and
9
. In all other cases, except these three, since
A_{\phi}
is trivial, we use the Langlands classification to find the corresponding admissible representation and note that
\Pi_{\phi}(G_{2}(F))\to\mathsf{Rep}(A_{\phi})
is trivial in these cases.
This is done case-by-case in Section
2.6
.
It then remains to consider only the Langlands parameters with infinitesimal parameters given by Cases
5
,
7
and
9
, specifically the three tempered (
i.e.
, bounded upon restriction to
W_{F}
) parameters denoted by
\phi_{\ref{infcase:4-D2}d}
,
\phi_{\ref{infcase:6-A2}d}
and
\phi_{\ref{infcase:8-sub}d}
. These three are of Arthur type and are lifted from Arthur parameters of elliptic endoscopic groups, as explained in Section
4
. We found the local Langlands correspondence for parameters with infinitesimal parameter
\lambda_{\ref{infcase:8-sub}}
in
[
10
]
*Theorem 2.5. The local Langlands correspondence for parameters with infinitesimal parameter
\lambda_{\ref{infcase:4-D2}}
and
\lambda_{\ref{infcase:6-A2}}
is obtained by similar arguments.

2.2.
Vogan’s version of the local Langlands correspondence

Let
\lambda
be an unramifed infinitesimal parameter of
G_{2}(F)
and let
\Pi_{\lambda}(G_{2}(F))
be the set of (equivalence classes of) irreducible smooth representations of
G_{2}(F)
with infinitesimal parameter
\lambda
. Associated with
\lambda
, we have the Vogan variety
V_{\lambda}
and the group
H_{\lambda}
. If
\phi
is an unramified Langlands parameter with infinitesimal parameter
\lambda
, let
C_{\pi}\subset V_{\lambda}
be the
H_{\lambda}
-orbit corresponding to
\phi
. For use below, we define
\dim(\phi){\,:=\,}\dim C_{\phi}
and
\dim(\pi){\,:=\,}\dim C_{\phi}
when
\phi
is the Langlands parameter for
\pi
.

Let
\Pi_{\phi}(G_{2}(F))
be the
L
-packet of
\phi
, whose existence was proved in
[
23
]
. Since
A_{\phi}
is both the component group of
\phi
and the equivariant fundamental group of
C_{\phi}
, the Langlands correspondence, as presented in Section
2.1
, now determines a bijection

\mathcal{L}:\Pi_{\phi}(G_{2}(F))\to\mathsf{Loc}_{H_{\lambda}}(C_{\phi})_{/\mathrm{iso}}^{\mathrm{simple}}

such that
\mathcal{L}(\pi)
corresponds to the representation
\langle\ ,\pi\rangle_{\phi}
of
A_{\phi}
.
Letting
\phi
run over all
L
-parameters with infinitesimal parameter
\lambda
, this defines a bijection

\mathcal{P}:\Pi_{\lambda}(G_{2}(F))\to\mathsf{Per}_{H_{\lambda}}(V_{\lambda})_{/\mathrm{iso}}^{\mathrm{simple}}

such that
\mathcal{P}(\pi)={\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}(\mathcal{L}(\pi))
. We make use of this bijection in Section
2.6
.
In Case
9
, this bijection was made explicit in
[
10
]
.

2.3.
Kazhdan-Lusztig conjecture

Using the case-by-case calculations of Section
2.6
, we have verified the Kazhdan-Lusztig conjecture, as stated in
[
9
]
*Section 10.3.3, for
G_{2}(F)
; this extends the result of
[
10
]
*Section 2.10.

2.4.
Aubert involution and the Fourier transform

We have also confirmed that the Aubert involution, as calculated in
[
25
]
, matches the Fourier transform of the corresponding simple perverse sheaves, using the bijection of Section
2.2
, for all unipotent representations of
G_{2}(F)
, confirming the expectation of
[
9
]
*Section 10.3.4 and extending the result of
[
10
]
*Section 2.13.

2.5.
A generalization of the component group of an Arthur parameter

We now introduce the algebraic group
\mathcal{S}^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
promised in the introduction.

Corollary 2.1
.

Let
\lambda:W_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G_{2}}}
be an unramified infinitesimal parameter for
G_{2}(F)
. Let
V_{\lambda}
be the moduli space of Langlands parameters with infinitesimal parameter
\lambda
. The conormal variety
\Lambda_{\lambda}
is a bundle of prehomogeneous vector spaces over
V_{\lambda}
.

Proof.

Infinitesimal parameters
\lambda
are classified by by the associated prehomogeneous vector space
V_{\lambda}
in Proposition
1.1
and these in turn are expressed in terms of the five prehomogeneous vector spaces in Proposition
1.2
. In Section
1.4
we saw that the conormal variety of each of these five prehomogeneous vector spaces all have the property that they’re bundles of prehomogeneous vector spaces.
∎

Definition 1
.

Notation as above.
Let
x
be the point for
\phi
in the moduli space
V
, where
\lambda
is the infinitesimal parameter of
\phi
.
Let
\Lambda_{\lambda}
be the conormal variety and recall, by Corollary
2.1
, that this is a bundle of prehomogeneous vector spaces above
V_{\lambda}
.
Set
\mathcal{S}^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}{\,:=\,}Z_{{\widehat{G_{2}}}}(x,y)
, where
x\in V
is a point in the moduli space
V
that maps to the Langlands parameter
\phi
and for
y\in\Lambda_{x}^{\text{sreg}}
. This variety is independent of the choice of
y
.
Also set
A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}{\,:=\,}\pi_{0}(\mathcal{S}^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi})
, equipped with the natural homomorphism
\mathcal{S}^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}\to A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
.
It follows immediately from
[
9
]
*Prop. 6.1.1,
\phi
is of Arthur type
\psi
, then
\mathcal{S}^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}=\mathcal{S}_{\psi}
.

Definition
1
is needed to find the ABV-packet coefficients
\langle\ ,\ \rangle:\mathcal{S}^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}\times\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}(G_{2}(F))\to{\bar{\mathbb{Q}}}
, which we do in the next section.

2.6.
Calculation of ABV-packet coefficients

In this section we calculate
\pi\mapsto\langle,\pi\rangle
for every unipotent representation
\pi
of
G_{2}(F)
.
The results are summarized in Table
2.6.1
, in which characters of
A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
are listed in the column with
\phi
at the top, classified by infinitesimal parameters. We list the endoscopic groups for
\phi
above
\phi
.
ABV-packets
\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}(G_{2}(F))
are found by gathering together the representations
\pi
for which the character of
A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
is non-zero.
