Definition 2
.

Let
\phi
be an unramified Langlands parameter for
G_{2}(F)
.
Following
[
9
]
, we set

\Pi_{\phi}^{{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}}(G_{2}(F)){\,:=\,}\{\pi\in\Pi_{\lambda}(G_{2}/F)|~\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}_{C_{\phi}}(\mathcal{P}(\pi))\neq 0\}.

For
s\in\mathcal{S}^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
and
\pi\in\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}(G_{2}(F))
, ABV-packet coefficients are given by

(5)

\langle s,\pi\rangle{\,:=\,}\trace_{s}\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}_{C_{\phi}}\mathcal{P}(\pi).

In this definition we make implicit use of the homomorphism
\mathcal{S}^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}\to A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
.

Let
\lambda:W_{F}\to\widehat{G_{2}}
be an unramified infinitesimal parameter for
G_{2}(F)
.
Then
\lambda
is determined by
\lambda({\rm{Fr}})=\widehat{m}(u_{1}q^{a_{1}},u_{2}q^{a_{2}})
where
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
are unitary complex numbers and
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
are real numbers.
Then

\displaystyle\lambda(w)

\displaystyle=\widehat{m}(u_{1}^{\ord(w)},u_{2}^{\ord(w)})\cdot\widehat{m}(|w|^{a_{1}},|w|^{a_{2}})

\displaystyle=(2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}^{\vee}+{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}^{\vee})(u_{2}^{\ord(w)}|w|^{a_{2}})\cdot({{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}^{\vee}+{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}^{\vee})((u_{1}u_{2}^{-1})^{\ord(w)}|w|^{a_{2}-a_{1}}).

Let
\chi=\chi_{1}\otimes\chi_{2}:T\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
be the dual character of
\lambda:W_{F}\to{\widehat{T}}
. By the above form of
\lambda
, we know that
\chi_{1}=\mu_{2}\nu^{a_{2}}
and
\chi_{2}=\mu_{1}\mu_{2}^{-1}\nu^{a_{1}-a_{2}}
,
i.e.
,
\chi
is given by

(6)

\chi=\mu_{2}\nu^{a_{2}}\otimes\mu_{1}\mu_{2}^{-1}\nu^{a_{1}-a_{2}},

where
\mu_{i}
is the unitary unramified character of
F^{\times}
defined by
\mu_{i}(\varpi)=u_{i}
.

(Case
1
)

Suppose
\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G_{2}}}
is a Langlands parameter with infinitesimal parameter
\lambda_{\text{\ref{infcase:0}}}
corresponding to Case
1
from Proposition
1.1
.
Then
\lambda_{\text{\ref{infcase:0}}}:W_{F}\to\widehat{G_{2}}
is an unramified infinitesimal parameter such that
\dim V_{\lambda_{\text{\ref{infcase:0}}}}=0
, so
{\hat{\gamma}}(\lambda_{\text{\ref{infcase:0}}}({\operatorname{Fr}}))\neq q
for every root
{\hat{\gamma}}\in{\hat{R}}
; this is Case
1
from Proposition
1.1
.
If we write
\lambda_{\text{\ref{infcase:0}}}({\operatorname{Fr}})={\hat{m}}(u_{1}q^{a}_{1},u_{2}q^{a_{2}})
then this case is equivalent to the condition

\{u_{2}^{2}u_{1}^{-1}q^{2a_{2}-a_{1}},u_{1}u_{2}^{-1}q^{a_{1}-a_{2}},u_{2}q^{a_{2}},u_{1}q^{a_{1}},u_{1}^{2}u_{2}^{-1}q^{2a_{1}-a_{2}},u_{1}u_{2}q^{a_{1}+a_{2}}\}\cap\{q,q^{-1}\}=\emptyset.

In this case
H_{\lambda_{\text{\ref{infcase:0}}}}
is the dual of an endoscopic group for
\widehat{G_{2}}
; so, besides
\widehat{G_{2}}
itself,
H_{\lambda_{\text{\ref{infcase:0}}}}
is
\SL_{3}
or
\SO_{4}
or one of the Levi subgroups
\GL_{2}^{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}}
,
\GL_{2}^{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}}
or
{\widehat{T}}
.
For each such
\lambda_{\text{\ref{infcase:0}}}
, there is a unique
\phi
with
\lambda_{\phi}=\lambda_{\text{\ref{infcase:0}}}
,
i.e.
,
\phi(w,x)=\lambda_{\text{\ref{infcase:0}}}(w)
.
In this case, there is a unique Langlands parameter with infinitesimal parameter
\lambda_{\ref{infcase:0}}
which is defined by
\phi_{\ref{infcase:0}}(w,x)=\lambda_{\ref{infcase:0}}(w)
.
If
a_{1}=a_{2}=0
, then
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
are unitary. Notice that there is a unique order 2 unramified character of
F^{\times}
and thus
\chi_{1},\chi_{2}
cannot be two distinct order 2 unramified characters. In this case,
I(\chi_{1}\otimes\chi_{2})
is irreducible by
[
20
]
.
If one of
a_{1},a_{2}
is nonzero, then at least one of
\chi_{1},\chi_{2}
is non-unitary. One easily translates the condition
\hat{\gamma}(\lambda_{\ref{infcase:0}}({\rm{Fr}}))\neq q^{\pm 1},\forall\hat{\gamma}\in R(\widehat{G_{2}},{\widehat{T}})
to the conditions on
\chi_{1},\chi_{2}
, which are exactly the conditions of
[
25
]
*Proposition 3.1. Thus, the induced representation
I(\chi_{1}\otimes\chi_{2})
is also irreducible by
[
25
]
*Proposition 3.1.
In both cases, the unramified local Langlands correspondence says that
\pi(\phi_{\ref{infcase:0}})=I(\chi_{1}\otimes\chi_{2})
and the
L
-packet is a singleton.
Here,
\phi_{\ref{infcase:0}}(w,x)=\lambda_{\ref{infcase:0}}(w)
, given above.
There are no coronal representations to the admissible representation
I(\chi_{1}\otimes\chi_{2})
; accordingly, this representation is its own ABV-packet in this case.
This is a direct consequence of the simple observation that there is but one simple object in
\mathsf{Per}_{H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:0}}}}(V_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:0}}})
; see also
1
.

(Case
2
)

Suppose
\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G_{2}}}
is a Langlands parameter with infinitesimal parameter
\lambda_{\text{\ref{infcase:1-short}}}
corresponding to Case
2
from Proposition
1.1
.
Then
V_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:1-short}}}=\mathrm{Span}\{X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}}\}
and
({{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}})(\lambda_{\ref{infcase:1-short}}({\operatorname{Fr}}))=q
and
{\hat{\gamma}}(\lambda_{\ref{infcase:1-short}}({\operatorname{Fr}}))\neq q
for every
{\hat{\gamma}}\in{\hat{R}}-\{\widehat{\gamma}_{1}+2\widehat{\gamma}_{2}\}
; in other words, if we write
\lambda_{\ref{infcase:1-short}}({\operatorname{Fr}})=(u_{1}q^{a_{1}},u_{2}q^{a_{2}})
, then
u_{1}u_{2}=1
,
a_{1}+a_{2}=1
and

\{u^{-3}q^{2-3a},u^{2}q^{2a-1},u^{-1}q^{1-a},uq^{a},u^{3}q^{3a-1}\}\cap\{q,q^{-1}\}=\emptyset,

where
u{\,:=\,}u_{1}
and
a{\,:=\,}a_{1}
and
H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:1-short}}}={\widehat{T}}
if
u^{2}q^{2a-1}\neq 1
and
H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:1-short}}}=\GL_{2}^{\hat{\gamma}_{1}}
if
u^{2}q^{2a-1}=1
.

The action of
H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:1-short}}}
on
V_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:1-short}}}
is given by

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\widehat{m}(x,y).rX_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}}&=&xrX_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}},&\textrm{ if }u^{2}q^{2a-1}\neq 1,\\
g.rX_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}}&=&\det(g)rX_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}},&\textrm{ if }u^{2}q^{2a-1}=1.\end{array}

This action has two orbits: the closed (zero) orbit and the open orbit. The corresponding Langlands parameters are explicitly given by

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\phi_{\ref{infcase:1-short}a}(w,x)&=&\lambda_{\ref{infcase:1-short}}(w),&\text{closed};\\
\phi_{\ref{infcase:1-short}b}(w,x)&=&\widehat{m}(1,u^{\ord(w)}|w|^{a-1/2})\iota_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}}(x),&\text{open}.\end{array}

The simple objects in
\mathsf{Per}_{H}(V)
are described in
2
using the base-change argument in the proof of Proposition
1.2
.

By Equation (
6
), the dual character of
\lambda_{\ref{infcase:1-short}}
is
\chi=\mu\nu^{a}\otimes\mu^{-1}\nu^{1-a}
, where
\mu:F^{\times}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{1}
is the unramified unitary character determined by
\mu(\varpi)=u
.
We consider the representation
I(\mu\nu^{a}\otimes\mu^{-1}\nu^{1-a})
. In the Grothendieck group, we have

\displaystyle I(\mu\nu^{a}\otimes\mu^{-1}\nu^{1-a})

\displaystyle=I(\mu\nu^{a-1}\otimes\nu)

\displaystyle=I_{\gamma_{2}}(a-1/2,I^{\GL_{2}}(\mu\nu^{1/2}\otimes\mu\nu^{-1/2}))

\displaystyle=I_{\gamma_{2}}(a-1/2,\mu\otimes\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}})+I_{\gamma_{2}}(a-1/2,\mu\circ\det).

By
[
25
]
*Theorem 3.1 (ii), the two representations,
I_{\gamma_{2}}(a-1/2,\mu\otimes\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}})
and
I_{\gamma_{2}}(a-1/2,\mu\circ\det)
, are irreducible.
For these representations, the local Langlands correspondence is given by

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\Pi_{\phi_{\ref{infcase:1-short}a}}(G_{2}(F))&=&\{I_{\gamma_{2}}(a-1/2,\mu\circ\det)\},\\
\Pi_{\phi_{\ref{infcase:1-short}b}}(G_{2}(F))&=&\{I_{\gamma_{2}}(a-1/2,\mu\otimes\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}})\}.\end{array}

It follows that the equivariant perverse sheaf corresponding to
I_{\gamma_{2}}(a-1/2,\mu\circ\det)
is
{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{\ref{infcase:1-short}a}})
, where
{\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{\ref{infcase:1-short}a}}
is the constant local system on
C_{\ref{infcase:1-short}a}=\{0\}
in
V=\mathbb{A}^{1}
, and the equivariant perverse sheaf corresponding to
I_{\gamma_{2}}(a-1/2,\mu\otimes\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}})
is
{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{\ref{infcase:1-short}b}})
, where
{\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{\ref{infcase:1-short}b}}
is the constant local system on the open orbit
C_{\ref{infcase:1-short}b}
.
Taking the case
n=1
of Section
2
and then using the base-change argument in the proof of Proposition
1.2
, it follows that the simple objects in
\mathsf{Per}_{H_{\lambda}}(V_{\lambda})
are
{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{\ref{infcase:1-short}a}})
and
{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{\ref{infcase:1-short}b}})
only, and that
\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}_{C_{\ref{infcase:1-short}a}}{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{\ref{infcase:1-short}a}})={\mathbbm{1}}_{\Lambda_{C_{\ref{infcase:1-short}a}}}
and
\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}_{C_{\ref{infcase:1-short}b}}{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{\ref{infcase:1-short}b}})={\mathbbm{1}}_{\Lambda_{C_{\ref{infcase:1-short}b}}}
while
\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}_{C_{\ref{infcase:1-short}a}}{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{\ref{infcase:1-short}b}})=0
and
\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}_{C_{\ref{infcase:1-short}b}}{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{\ref{infcase:1-short}a}})=0
.
Therefore, there are no coronal representations for the
L
-packets above, and we have

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi_{\ref{infcase:1-short}a}}(G_{2}(F))&=&\Pi_{\phi_{\ref{infcase:1-short}a}}(G_{2}(F)),\\
\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi_{\ref{infcase:1-short}b}}(G_{2}(F))&=&\Pi_{\phi_{\ref{infcase:1-short}b}}(G_{2}(F)).\end{array}

These calculations follow from the results of Section
2
.

(Case
3
)

Suppose
\phi
is a Langlands parameter with infinitesimal parameter
\lambda_{\ref{infcase:2-long}}
from Case
3
of Proposition
1.1
.
This case is quite similar to the previous case.
The infinitesimal parameter
\lambda_{\ref{infcase:2-long}}
can be represented by
\lambda_{\ref{infcase:2-long}}({\operatorname{Fr}})=\widehat{m}(uq^{a},u^{-1}q^{1-a})
with
u\in\mathbb{C}^{1},a\in\mathbb{R}
satisfying
\widehat{\gamma}(\lambda_{\ref{infcase:2-long}}({\operatorname{Fr}})=\widehat{m}(uq^{a},u^{-1}q^{1-a}))\neq q
for all
\widehat{\gamma}\in\widehat{R}-\{2\widehat{\gamma}_{1}+3\widehat{\gamma}_{2}\}
, which is equivalent to

\{u^{-3}q^{2-3a},u^{2}q^{2a-1},u^{-1}q^{1-a},uq^{a},u^{3}q^{3a-1}\}\cap\{q,q^{-1}\}=\emptyset.

We have
V_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:2-long}}}=\mathrm{Span}\{X_{2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+3{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}}\}
.
In this case,
H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:2-long}}}={\widehat{T}}
if
u^{2}q^{2a-1}\neq 1
, and
H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:2-long}}}=\GL_{2,{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}}
if
u^{2}q^{2a-1}=1
. The action of
H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:2-long}}}
on
V_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:2-long}}}
is given by

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\widehat{m}(x,y).rX_{2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+3{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}}&=&xyrX_{2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+3{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}},&\textrm{ if }u^{2}q^{2a-1}\neq 1,\\
g.rX_{2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+3{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}}&=&\det(g)rX_{2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+3{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}},&\textrm{ if }u^{2}q^{2a-1}=1.\end{array}

This action has two orbits – the zero orbit and the open orbit – and each Langlands parameter has a trivial component group.
Representatives for the two
L
-parameters are

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\phi_{\ref{infcase:2-long}b}(w,x)&=&\lambda_{\ref{infcase:2-long}}(w)\\
\phi_{\ref{infcase:2-long}b}(w,x)&=&\widehat{m}(u^{\ord(w)}|w|^{a-1/2},u^{-\ord(w)}|w|^{-a+1/2})\cdot\iota_{2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+3{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}}(x).\end{array}

By Equation (
6
), the dual character of
\lambda_{\ref{infcase:2-long}}
is
\mu^{-1}\nu^{1-a}\otimes\mu^{2}\nu^{2a-1},
where
\mu
is the unitary unramified character of
F^{\times}
determined by
\mu(\varpi)=u
. In the Grothendieck group, we have

\displaystyle I(\mu^{-1}\nu^{1-a}\otimes\mu^{2}\nu^{2a-1})

\displaystyle=I(\mu\nu^{a}\otimes\mu\nu^{a-1})

\displaystyle=I_{\gamma_{1}}(a-1/2,I^{\GL_{2}}(\mu\nu^{1/2}\otimes\mu\nu^{-1/2}))

\displaystyle=I_{\gamma_{1}}(a-1/2,\mu\otimes\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}})+I_{\gamma_{1}}(a-1/2,\mu\circ\det).

By
[
25
]
*Theorem 3.1 (i), the representations
I_{\gamma_{1}}(a-1/2,\mu\otimes\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}})
and
I_{\gamma_{1}}(a-1/2,\mu\cdot\det)
are irreducible.
The Langlands correspondence is given in given in Table
2.7.1
.
Arguing as in Case
2
or using our microlocal analysis of the prehomogeneous vector space
2
, the ABV-packet coefficients are given in Table
2.6.1
.

(Case
4
)

Suppose
\phi
is an unramified Langlands parameter with infinitesimal parameter
\lambda_{\ref{infcase:3}}
from Case
4
of Proposition
1.1
.
We can take
\lambda_{\ref{infcase:3}}({\operatorname{Fr}})=\widehat{m}(\theta q^{1/3},\theta^{2}q^{2/3})
with
\theta=\theta_{3}^{n}
for
n=0,1,2
and
H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:3}}}=\GL_{2}^{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+3{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}}
.
To see this note that
{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}(\lambda_{\ref{infcase:3}}({\operatorname{Fr}}))=q
and
(2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+3{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}})(\lambda({\operatorname{Fr}}))=q
and
{\hat{\gamma}}(\lambda_{\ref{infcase:3}}({\operatorname{Fr}}))\neq q
for every other
{\hat{\gamma}}\in{\hat{R}}
, so if
\lambda_{\ref{infcase:3}}({\operatorname{Fr}})={\widehat{m}}(u_{1}q^{a_{1}},u_{2}q^{a_{2}})
then

u_{2}=u_{1}^{2}\in\{1,\theta_{3},\theta_{3}^{2}\}
and
a_{1}=\frac{1}{3}
,
a_{2}=\frac{2}{3}
.
The action of
H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:3}}}
on
V_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:3}}}
is
\det\otimes\Sym^{1}
, which is to say,
h\cdot x=\det(h)hx
where
hx
is matrix multiplication.
This is case
3
for
n=1
.
In particular, there are two orbits of this action: the zero orbit and the open orbit. The corresponding Langlands parameters are given by

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\phi_{\ref{infcase:3}a}(w,x)&=&\lambda_{\ref{infcase:3}}(w),\\
\phi_{\ref{infcase:3}b}(w,x)&=&\widehat{m}(\theta^{\ord(w)}|w|^{1/3},\theta^{2\ord(w)}|w|^{1/6})\cdot\iota_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}}(x).\end{array}

By Equation (
6
), the dual character of
\lambda_{\ref{infcase:3}}
is
\chi=\vartheta\nu^{2/3}\otimes\vartheta^{2}\nu^{-1/3}
where
\vartheta
is the unramified character of
F^{\times}
dual to
\theta
. In the Grothendieck group, we have

\displaystyle I(\theta_{3}^{n}\nu^{2/3}\otimes\theta_{3}^{2n}\nu^{-1/3})

\displaystyle=I_{\gamma_{1}}(1/6,I^{\GL_{2}}(\theta_{3}^{n}\nu^{1/2}\otimes\theta_{3}^{-n}\nu^{-1/2}))

\displaystyle=I_{{\gamma_{1}}}(1/6,\theta_{3}^{n}\otimes\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}})+I_{\gamma_{1}}(1/6,\theta^{n}_{3}\circ\det).

The Langlands correspondence is given in given in Table
2.7.1
.
Using our microlocal analysis of the prehomogeneous vector space
3
, for
n=1
, the ABV-packet coefficients are given in Table
2.6.1
.

(Case
5
)

Let
\lambda_{\ref{infcase:4-D2}}
be the infinitesimal parameter appearing in
Case
5
of Proposition
1.1
.
Then
\lambda_{\ref{infcase:4-D2}}({\operatorname{Fr}})=\widehat{m}(q,-q)
and
V_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:4-D2}}}=\mathrm{Span}\{X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}},X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}}\}
and
H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:4-D2}}}={\widehat{T}}
. The action of
{\widehat{T}}
on
V_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:4-D2}}}
is given by

\widehat{m}(x,y).(r_{1}X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}}+r_{2}X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}})=y^{2}x^{-1}r_{1}X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}}+xr_{2}X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}}.

There are 4 orbits:
C_{\ref{infcase:4-D2}a}=\{0\}
,
C_{\ref{infcase:4-D2}b}=\{r_{1}X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}}{\ |\ }r_{1}\neq 0\}
,
C_{\ref{infcase:4-D2}c}=\{r_{2}X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}}{\ |\ }r_{2}\neq 0\}
and
C_{\ref{infcase:4-D2}d}=\{r_{1}X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}}+r_{2}X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}}{\ |\ }r_{1},r_{2}\neq 0\}
.
We found
\mathsf{Per}_{H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:4-D2}}}}(V_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:4-D2}}})
in Proposition
1.2
.
In particular, there are four
H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:4-D2}}}
-orbits in
V_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:4-D2}}}
:
C_{\text{\ref{infcase:4-D2}a}}=\{0\}
,
C_{\text{\ref{infcase:4-D2}b}}=\{r_{1}X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}}{\ |\ }r_{1}\neq 0\}
,
C_{\text{\ref{infcase:4-D2}c}}=\{r_{2}X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}}{\ |\ }r_{2}\neq 0\}
and
C_{\text{\ref{infcase:4-D2}d}}=\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0ptV}\limits^{{}_{\circ}}}=\{r_{1}X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}}+r_{2}X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}}{\ |\ }r_{1},r_{2}\neq 0\}
.
Corresponding Langlands parameters are given by

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\phi_{\text{\ref{infcase:4-D2}a}}(w,x)&=&\lambda(w),\\
\phi_{\text{\ref{infcase:4-D2}b}}(w,x)&=&\widehat{m}(|w|,(-1)^{\ord(w)}|w|^{1/2})\cdot\iota_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}}(x),\\
\phi_{\text{\ref{infcase:4-D2}c}}(w,x)&=&\widehat{m}(1,(-1)^{\ord(w)}|w|^{1/2})\cdot\iota_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}}(x),\\
\phi_{\text{\ref{infcase:4-D2}d}}(w,x)&=&\widehat{m}(1,(-1)^{\ord(w)})\iota_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}}(x)\iota_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+2{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}}(x).\end{array}

Note that we have
A_{\phi_{\ref{infcase:4-D2}d}}=\langle{\widehat{m}}(1,-1)\rangle{\ \cong\ }\langle\theta_{2}\rangle
and the component groups of the other 3 Langlands parameters are trivial.

The dual character of
\lambda_{\ref{infcase:4-D2}}
is
\theta_{2}\nu\otimes\theta_{2}
by Equation (
6
).
Recall that
\theta_{2}
is the unique unramified order 2 character of
F^{\times}
.
By
[
25
]
*Proposition 4.1, we have

(7)

\displaystyle\begin{split}I(\theta_{2}\nu\otimes\theta_{2})&=I_{\gamma_{1}}(1/2,\theta_{2}\otimes\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}})+I_{\gamma_{1}}(1/2,\theta_{2}\circ\det)\\
&=I_{\gamma_{2}}(1/2,\theta_{2}\otimes\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}})+I_{{\gamma_{2}}}(1/2,\theta_{2}\circ\det),\end{split}

and

(8)

\begin{split}\begin{array}[]{rcl}I_{\gamma_{1}}(1/2,\theta_{2}\otimes\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}})&=&\pi(\theta_{2})+J_{\gamma_{1}}(1/2,\theta_{2}\otimes\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}}),\\
I_{{\gamma_{2}}}(1/2,\theta_{2}\otimes\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}})&=&\pi(\theta_{2})+J_{\gamma_{2}}(1/2,\theta_{2}\otimes\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}}),\\
I_{\gamma_{1}}(1/2,\theta_{2}\circ\det)&=&J_{\gamma_{2}}(1,I^{\GL_{2}}(1\otimes\theta_{2}))+J_{\gamma_{2}}(1/2,\theta_{2}\otimes\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}}),\\
I_{\gamma_{2}}(1/2,\theta_{2}\circ\det)&=&J_{\gamma_{2}}(1,I^{\GL_{2}}(1\otimes\theta_{2}))+J_{\gamma_{1}}(1/2,\theta_{2}\otimes\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}}),\end{array}\end{split}

As in
[
25
]
, we write
\pi(\theta_{2})
for the unique irreducible sub-representation of
I(\theta_{2}\nu\otimes\theta_{2})
; note that
\pi(\theta_{2})
is square integrable by
[
25
]
*Proposition 4.1.
and all of the above equations are in the Grothendieck group of representations of
G_{2}(F)
.

The proof of the Langlands correspondence
\Pi_{\phi}(G_{2}(F))\to\widehat{A_{\phi}}
for all unramified Langlands parameters for
G_{2}(F)
with infinitesimal parameter
\lambda_{\ref{infcase:4-D2}}
is similar to that of
[
10
]
*Theorem 2.5, which follows from some well-known facts.
The Langlands correspondence is given in Table
2.1.1
; see also Table
2.7.1
.
Here we only give some details of the proof of
\pi(\phi_{\text{\ref{infcase:4-D2}d}},\vartheta_{2})=I_{0}(G_{2}[-1]){\,:=\,}\mathrm{cInd}_{G_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{F})}^{G_{2}(F)}(G_{2}[-1])
.
Using
[
22
]
*p.270, the local system
(\phi_{\text{\ref{infcase:4-D2}d}},\vartheta_{2})
is cuspidal and thus
\pi(\phi_{\text{\ref{infcase:4-D2}d}},\vartheta_{2})
is a depth zero supercuspidal representation of the form

I_{0}(\sigma){\,:=\,}\mathrm{cInd}_{G_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{F})}^{G_{2}(F)}(\sigma),

where
\sigma
is a unipotent cuspidal representation of
G_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
.
There are exactly four unipotent cuspidal representations of
G_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
; see
[
7
]
*p.460.
To determine which one the representation
\sigma
is, we use the formal degree conjecture of
[
19
]
, which is known in this case by
[
11
]
.
A simple calculation shows that the
L
-factor is given by

L(s,\phi_{\text{\ref{infcase:4-D2}d}},\Ad)=\frac{1}{(1+q^{-2-s})(1+a^{-1-s})(1-q^{-1-s})^{2}},

and the epsilon
\epsilon(s,\phi_{\text{\ref{infcase:4-D2}d}},\Ad)=q^{10(1/2-s)}.
Thus

\gamma(0,\phi_{\text{\ref{infcase:4-D2}d}},\Ad)=\frac{q^{9}}{(q+1)(q^{3}+1)}.

Here the
\epsilon
-factor and the
\gamma
-factor are computed with respect to a fixed additive character of
F
, which is omitted from the notation.
The formal degree conjecture asserts that

\frac{\deg(\sigma)}{\mu(G_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{F}))}=\frac{\dim(\vartheta_{2})}{|A_{\phi_{\text{\ref{infcase:4-D2}d}}}|}|\gamma(0,\phi_{\text{\ref{infcase:4-D2}d}},\Ad)|,

where
\mu(G_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{F}))
is the Haar measure of
G_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{F})
normalized as in
[
19
]
,
i.e.
,

\mu(G_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{F}))=q^{-\dim(G_{2}(F))}\cdot|G_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{q})|=q^{-8}(q^{6}-1)(q^{2}-1).

Thus we get

\deg(\sigma)=\frac{q(q^{6}-1)(q^{2}-1)}{(q^{3}+1)(q+1)}.

Comparing it with the 4 unipotent cupsidal representations of
G_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
, we get
\sigma=G_{2}[-1]
.

Using the microlocal analysis of the prehomogeneous vector space
4
for
n=2
from Section
1.4
, the ABV-packet coefficients are given by in Table
2.6.1
.

. The action of

on

\GL_{2}^{\hat{\gamma}_{1}}
with
\GL_{2}
.
Category
\mathsf{Per}_{H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:5}}}}(V_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:5}}}){\ \cong\ }\mathsf{Per}_{\GL_{2}}(\mathbb{A}^{2})
, with reference to the prehomogeneous vector space
3
for

.
In particular, this action has two orbits: the zero orbit

.

\phi_{\ref{infcase:5}b}(w,x)&=&\widehat{m}(|w|^{3/2},|w|^{3/2})\cdot\iota_{\hat{\gamma}_{2}}(x).\end{array}

By Equation (
6
), the dual character of

is

. In the Grothendieck group, we have

\displaystyle I(\nu\otimes\nu)

\displaystyle=I_{\gamma_{2}}(3/2,\pi(\nu^{1/2},\nu^{-1/2}))

\displaystyle=I_{\gamma_{2}}(3/2,\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}})+I_{\gamma_{2}}(3/2,1_{\GL_{2}}).

(Case

The ABV-packets and coefficients for Langlands parameters with infinitesimal parameter
\lambda_{\ref{infcase:6-A2}}

and the action of

\widehat{m}(x,y).(r_{1}X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}}+r_{2}X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+3{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}})=y^{2}x^{-1}r_{1}X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}}+x^{2}y^{-1}r_{2}X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+3{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}}.

Using the base-change argument in the proof of Proposition
1.2
we find that
\mathsf{Per}_{H_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:6-A2}}}}(V_{\lambda_{\ref{infcase:6-A2}}})
is equivalent to the category of equivariant perverse sheaves on the prehomogeneous vector space
4
for
n=3
.
There are 4 orbits of this action:
C_{\ref{infcase:6-A2}a}=\{0\},C_{\ref{infcase:6-A2}b}=\{r_{1}X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}}{\ |\ }r_{1}\neq 0\}
,
C_{\ref{infcase:6-A2}c}=\{r_{2}X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+3{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}},r_{2}\neq 0\}
and

.
Corresponding Langlands parameters are given by

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\phi_{\ref{infcase:6-A2}a}(w,x)&=&\lambda(w),\\
\phi_{\ref{infcase:6-A2}b}(w,x)&=&\widehat{m}(\theta_{3}^{\ord(w)}|w|,\theta_{3}^{2\ord(w)}|w|^{1/2})\cdot\iota_{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}(x),\\
\phi_{\ref{infcase:6-A2}c}(w,x)&=&\widehat{m}(\theta_{3}^{\ord(w)}|w|^{1/2},\theta_{3}^{2\ord(w)}|w|)\cdot\iota_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+3{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}}(x),\\
\phi_{\ref{infcase:6-A2}d}(w,x)&=&\widehat{m}(\theta_{3}^{\ord(w)},\theta_{3}^{2\ord(w)})\varphi_{X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}}+X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+3{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}}}(x),\end{array}

where
\varphi_{X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}}+X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+3{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}}}(x):\SL_{2}(\mathbb{C})\to\widehat{G_{2}}
is a group homomorphism determined by the
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
-triple
(e,f,h)
with
e=X_{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+X_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+3{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}}
,
f=2X_{-{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}}+2X_{-({{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+3{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}})}
and
h=2H_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}}+2H_{{{\hat{\gamma}}_{1}}+3{{\hat{\gamma}}_{2}}}
.
We find that
A_{\phi_{\ref{infcase:6-A2}d}}
has order
3
and the component groups of the other 3 parameters are trivial.

The local Langlands correspondence in this case is obtained by arguments similar to the proof in case

and is given in Table

for
