Properties
1
,
2
,
3
and
4
are elementary for
\SO_{4}(F)
and its pure inner forms.
Proposition
2
is interesting for
\SO_{4}(F)
and its pure inner forms.

Proposition 3.2
.

The distributions
\Theta^{\SO_{4}}_{\phi}
(resp.
\Theta^{\SO_{4}^{\delta}}_{\phi}
) form a basis for the space of invariant distributions spanned by characters of unipotent irreducible admissible representations
\pi
of
\SO_{4}(F)
(resp.
\SO_{4}^{\delta}(F)
).
The distributions
\Theta^{\SO_{4}}_{\phi}
and
\Theta^{\SO_{4}^{\delta}}_{\phi}
are stable.

Proof.

In all cases except
\phi=\phi_{\ref{ssec:SO4}.0^{\prime}}
,
\Pi_{\phi}(\SO_{4}(F))
and
\Pi_{\phi}(\SO^{\delta}_{4}(F))
are singletons (or empty) and
\Theta^{\SO_{4}}_{\phi}=\Theta_{\pi}
.
To see that these are all stable distributions, we may argue as follows.

In Case
3.4
.0, the representations
I^{\SO_{4}}(\chi_{1}\otimes\chi_{2})
are stable because they are standard modules, stably induced from representations of
T(F)
.

In Case
3.4
.1 we have

\displaystyle\Theta_{I^{\SO_{4}}(\chi\nu^{1/2}\otimes\chi\nu^{-1/2})}=\Theta_{I_{P_{1}}(\chi\otimes\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}})}+\Theta_{I_{P_{1}}(\chi\circ\det)}.

Since the distribution
\Theta_{I^{\SO_{4}}(\chi\nu^{1/2}\otimes\chi\nu^{-1/2})}
is stable (by an argument as above) and
\Theta_{I_{P_{1}}(\chi\circ\det)}
is stable by hand,
\Theta_{I_{P_{1}}(\chi\circ\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}})}
is also stable.
For Case
3.4
.2 argue as in Case
3.4
.1.
In Case
3.4
.3 we have

\Theta_{I^{\SO_{4}}(\chi\nu\otimes\chi)}=\Theta_{J_{\emptyset}(\chi\nu\otimes\chi)}+\Theta_{J_{P_{1}}(1/2,\chi\otimes\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}})}+\Theta_{J_{P_{2}}(1/2,\chi\otimes\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}})}+\Theta_{\mathrm{St}_{\SO_{4}}(\chi)}.

Since
I^{\SO_{4}}(\chi\nu\otimes\chi)
is stable (argue as in Case
3.4
.0) and
J_{\emptyset}(\chi\nu\otimes\chi)
,
J_{P_{1}}(1/2,\chi\otimes\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}})
, and
J_{P_{2}}(1/2,\chi\otimes\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}})
are stable (argue as in Case
3.4
.1) it follows that
\Theta_{\mathrm{St}_{\SO_{4}}(\chi)}
is stable.
Finally,
\chi_{\SO_{4}^{\delta}(F)}
is stable because
{\mathbbm{1}}_{\SO_{4}^{\delta}(F)}
is stable.
Case
\phi=\phi_{\ref{ssec:SO4}.0^{\prime}}
is a well known:
\Pi_{\phi_{\ref{ssec:SO4}.0^{\prime}}}(\SO_{4}(F))=\{\pi_{4},\pi^{\prime}_{4}\}
where
\pi_{4}
and
\pi_{4}^{\prime}
are the two irreducible representations appearing in the induced representation
I^{\SO_{4}}(\chi_{2}\otimes 1)
and the stable distribution attached to this
L
-packet is
\Theta^{\SO_{4}}_{\phi_{\ref{ssec:SO4}.0^{\prime}}}=\Theta_{\pi_{4}}+\Theta_{\pi^{\prime}_{4}}
.
∎

3.5.
Unipotent representations of
\PGL_{3}
and its pure inner forms

In this section we find the ABV-packets and ABV-packet coefficients for all unipotent representations of
\PGL_{3}
and its pure inner forms.

As in Section
3.3
, the category of unipotent representations of
\PGL_{3}(F)
is precisely the category of unramified principal series representations of
\PGL_{3}(F)
.
In this section we partition the set
\Pi(\PGL_{3}(F))_{\text{unip}}
of unipotent representations of
\PGL_{3}(F)
into
L
-packets and also describe the corresponding
L
-parameters.
We do the same for the non-split inner form
D^{\times}/F^{\times}
, where
D
is a central division algebra of degree
3
over
F
.
The
p
-adic group
\PGL_{3}
has three pure inner forms but only two inner forms:
H^{1}(F,\PGL_{3})
has cardinality while its image in

H^{1}(F,\PGL_{3})\to H^{1}(F,\text{Aut}(\PGL_{3}))

has cardinality
2
. We remark that this function of pointed sets is not surjective.
Let
\delta,\delta^{\prime}\in Z^{1}(F,\PGL_{3})
be representatives for the two non-trivial classes in
H^{1}(F,\PGL_{3})
.
Let
\PGL_{3}^{\delta},\PGL_{3}^{\delta^{\prime}}
be the inner forms of
\PGL_{3}
attached to these cocycles; then
\PGL_{3}^{\delta}{\ \cong\ }\PGL_{3}^{\delta^{\prime}}
as algebraic groups over
F
, although
[\delta]\neq[\delta^{\prime}]
in
H^{1}(F,\PGL_{3})
.
We can take
\PGL_{3}^{\delta}(F)=D^{\times}/F^{\times}
and
\PGL_{3}^{\delta^{\prime}}(F)=(D^{\prime})^{\times}/F^{\times}
, where
D^{\prime}
is the opposite algebra of
D
.

Langlands correspondence

Table 3.5.1.
Pure Arthur packets for unipotent representations of
\PGL_{3}(F)
and its pure inner forms
\PGL_{3}^{\delta}
and
\PGL_{3}^{\delta^{\prime}}
: each row gives a pure
L
-packet for an
L
-parameter and its coronal representations and therefore determines a pure Arthur packet. Notation is explained below. Cases, indicated in the left-hand column, gather representations together by infinitesimal parameter.

\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c| l |}\hline\cr\text{$L$-parameter}&\text{Pure $L$-packet}&\text{Coronal}&\text{Parameter of }\\
\phi&\Pi^{\text{pure}}_{\phi}(\PGL_{3}/F)&\text{representations}&\text{Arthur type?}\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.0}&\text{Ind}_{B_{3}(F)}^{\GL_{3}(F)}(\chi_{1}\otimes\chi_{2}\otimes\chi_{1}^{-1}\chi_{2}^{-1})&&\text{unitary $\chi_{1}$, $\chi_{2}$}\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.1a}&I_{\alpha_{1}}((\chi\circ\det)\otimes\chi^{-2})&&\text{unitary $\chi$}\\
\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.1b}&I_{\alpha_{1}}(\chi\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}}\otimes\chi^{-2})&&\text{unitary $\chi$}\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.2a}&I_{\alpha_{1}}((\chi\nu^{1/6}\circ\det)\otimes\chi\nu^{-1/3})&&\text{no}\\
\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.2b}&I_{\alpha_{1}}(\chi\nu^{1/6}\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}}\otimes\chi\nu^{-1/3})&&\text{no}\\
\hline\cr\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3a}&\chi_{\PGL_{3}}&\chi_{\PGL_{3}^{\delta}(F)},\ \chi_{\PGL_{3}^{\delta^{\prime}}(F)}&\text{yes}\\
\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3b}&\chi\otimes J_{\alpha_{1}}(\nu\otimes\nu^{-1/2}\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}})&\chi_{\PGL_{3}^{\delta}(F)},\ \chi_{\PGL_{3}^{\delta^{\prime}}(F)}&\text{no}\\
\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3c}&\chi\otimes J_{\alpha_{1}}(\nu^{1/2}\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}}\otimes\nu^{-1})&\chi_{\PGL_{3}^{\delta}(F)},\ \chi_{\PGL_{3}^{\delta^{\prime}}(F)}&\text{no}\\
\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3d}&\chi\otimes\mathrm{St}_{\PGL_{3}},\ \chi_{\PGL_{3}^{\delta}(F)},\ \chi_{\PGL_{3}^{\delta^{\prime}}(F)}&&\text{yes}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

Let
T
be the standard maximal torus in
\PGL_{3}
and let
B_{3}
be the standard Borel subgroup of
\GL_{3}
.
We denote the matching positive roots of
\GL_{3}
by
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{2}
. Let
P_{\alpha_{i}}
be the standard maximal parabolic subgroup of
\GL_{3}
such that the root space
\alpha_{i}
is in the Levi of
P_{\alpha_{i}}
.
Since
\PGL_{3}(F)=\GL_{3}(F)/F^{\times}
, a representation of
\PGL_{3}(F)
is just a representation of
\GL_{3}(F)
with trivial central character.
The cases treated below group representations together by infinitesimal parameter.

3.5
.0

Let
\chi_{1},\chi_{2}
are characters of
F^{\times}
such that
\chi_{1}\chi_{2}^{-1}
,
\chi_{1}^{2}\chi_{2}
and
\chi_{1}\chi_{2}^{2}
are not equal to
\nu
and not equal to
\nu^{-1}
. Then the induced representation

\text{Ind}_{B_{3}(F)}^{\GL_{3}(F)}(\chi_{1}\otimes\chi_{2}\otimes\chi_{1}^{-1}\chi_{2}^{-1})

is irreducible.
Note that this representation has trivial central character and thus can be viewed as a representation of
\PGL_{3}(F)
. We denote this representation of
\PGL_{3}(F)
by
I^{\PGL_{3}}(\chi_{1}\otimes\chi_{2})
. The corresponding Langlands parameter for
I^{\PGL_{3}}(\chi_{1}\otimes\chi_{2})
is given by

\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.0}(w,x)=\begin{pmatrix}\varphi_{1}(w)&0&0\\
0&\varphi_{2}(w)&0\\
0&0&\varphi_{1}(w)^{-1}\varphi_{2}(w)^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\in\SL_{3}(\mathbb{C}),

where
\varphi_{i}:W_{F}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
corresponds to
\chi_{i}:F^{\times}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
under class field theory.

3.5
.1

Let
\chi
be a character of
F^{\times}
with
\chi^{3}\neq\nu^{\pm 1/2},\nu^{\pm 3/2}
. We consider the representation

\text{Ind}_{B_{3}(F)}^{\GL_{3}(F)}(\chi\nu^{1/2}\otimes\chi\nu^{-1/2}\otimes\chi^{-2})

of
\GL_{3}(F)
, which is also viewed as a representation of
\PGL_{3}(F)
. This representation has length 2, and its two irreducible components are
I_{\alpha_{1}}(\chi\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}}\otimes\chi^{-2})
and
I_{\alpha_{1}}(\chi\circ\det\otimes\chi^{-2})
, which, adapting the notation above, are written as

I^{\PGL_{3}}_{\alpha_{1}}(\chi\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}}),\qquad\text{and}\qquad I^{\PGL_{3}}_{\alpha_{1}}(\chi\circ\det).

Here
I^{\PGL_{3}}_{\alpha_{1}}
stands for
\text{Ind}_{P_{\alpha_{1}}}^{\GL_{3}}
, and a representation of
\GL_{3}(F)
is viewed as a representation of
\PGL_{3}(F)
if it has trivial central character. The Langlands parameter of
I_{\alpha_{1}}(\chi\circ\det\otimes\chi^{-2})
is given by

\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.1.a}(w,x)=\begin{pmatrix}\varphi(w)|w|^{1/2}&0&0\\
0&\varphi(w)|w|^{-1/2}&0\\
0&0&\varphi(w)^{-2}\end{pmatrix}\in\SL_{3}(\mathbb{C}),

where
\varphi:W_{F}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
is the dual character of
\chi
. The Langlands parameter for
I_{\alpha_{1}}(\chi\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}}\otimes\chi^{-2})
is given by

\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.1b}\left(w,\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)=\begin{pmatrix}\varphi(w)a&\varphi(w)b&0\\
\varphi(w)c&\varphi(w)d&0\\
0&0&\varphi(w)^{-2}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\varphi(w)x&\\
&\varphi(w)^{-2}\end{pmatrix}\in\SL_{3}(\mathbb{C}).

Note that the infinitesimal parameter for these representations is

\lambda_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.1a}(w)=\begin{pmatrix}\varphi(w)|w|^{1/2}&0&0\\
0&\varphi(w)|w|^{-1/2}&0\\
0&0&\varphi(w)^{-2}\end{pmatrix}.

3.5
.2

Let
\chi
be a character of
F^{\times}
with
\chi^{3}=1
. Consider the representation

\text{Ind}_{B_{3}(F)}^{\GL_{3}(F)}(\chi\nu^{2/3}\otimes\chi\nu^{-1/3}\otimes\chi\nu^{-1/3}),

which is also viewed as a representation of
\PGL_{3}(F)
. This representation has length 2 and its two components are

I_{\alpha_{1}}(\chi\nu^{1/6}\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}}\otimes\chi\nu^{-1/3})\quad\text{and}\quad I_{\alpha_{1}}(\chi\nu^{1/6}\circ\det\otimes\chi\nu^{-1/3}).

The Langlands parameter of
I_{\alpha_{1}}(\chi\nu^{1/6}\circ\det\otimes\chi\nu^{-1/3})
is given by

\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.2a}(w,x)=\begin{pmatrix}\varphi(w)|w|^{2/3}&0&0\\
0&\varphi(w)|w|^{-1/3}&0\\
0&0&\varphi(w)|w|^{-1/3}\end{pmatrix}\in\SL_{3}(\mathbb{C}),

and the Langlands parameter of
I_{\alpha_{1}}(\chi\nu^{1/6}\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}}\otimes\chi\nu^{-1/3})
is given by

\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.2b}(w,x)=\begin{pmatrix}\varphi(w)|w|^{1/6}x&\\
&\varphi(w)|w|^{-1/3}\end{pmatrix}\in\SL_{3}(\mathbb{C}).

3.5
.3

Let
\chi
be a character of
F^{\times}
with
\chi^{3}=1
. Consider the induced representation

\text{Ind}_{B_{3}(F)}^{\GL_{3}(F)}(\chi\nu\otimes\chi\otimes\chi\nu^{-1})

of
\GL_{3}(F)
(and also of
\PGL_{3}(F)
). This representation has length 4 and its components are given by

\begin{array}[]{cc}\chi_{\PGL_{3}(F)},&\chi\otimes J_{\alpha_{2}}(\nu\otimes\nu^{-1/2}\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}}),\\
\chi\otimes J_{\alpha_{1}}(\nu^{1/2}\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}}\otimes\nu^{-1}),&\chi\otimes\mathrm{St}_{\PGL_{3}},\end{array}

where
\chi_{\PGL_{3}(F)}=\chi\circ\det
,
J_{\alpha_{1}}(\nu^{1/2}\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}}\otimes\nu^{-1})
is the unique quotient of
I_{\alpha_{1}}(\nu^{1/2}\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}}\otimes\nu^{-1})
and
J_{\alpha_{2}}(\nu\otimes\nu^{-1/2}\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}}
is defined similarly. The local Langlands parameters of these 4 representations are given, in order, by

\displaystyle\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3a}(w,x)

\displaystyle=\varphi(w)\begin{pmatrix}|w|&&\\
&1&\\
&&|w|^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\in\SL_{3}(\mathbb{C}),

\displaystyle\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3b}(w,x)

\displaystyle=\varphi(w)\begin{pmatrix}|w|&\\
&|w|^{-1/2}x\end{pmatrix}\in\SL_{3}(\mathbb{C}),

\displaystyle\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3c}(w,x)

\displaystyle=\varphi(w)\begin{pmatrix}|w|^{1/2}x&\\
&|w|^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\in\SL_{3}(\mathbb{C}),

\displaystyle\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3d}(w,x)

\displaystyle=\varphi(w)\Sym^{2}(x)\in\SL_{3}(\mathbb{C}),

where
\varphi:W_{F}\to C^{\times}
is Langlands parameter for the quadratic character
\chi
and
\Sym^{2}
is the symmetric square representation of
\SL_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.

This completes the description of the Langlands correspondence for unipotent representations of
\PGL_{3}(F)
.

ABV-packets

Table
3.5.1
presents the
L
-packets for all unipotent representations of
\PGL_{3}(F)
.
Returning to pure
L
-packets, we only explain the case
3.5
.3 since the others proceed by similar, simpler arguments.
Denote the last Langlands parameter in Case
3.5
.3 by
{\phi}_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3d}
. This Langlands parameter has component group
\mu_{3}
so its pure
L
-packet contains 3 admissible irreducible representations:

\Pi_{{\phi}_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3d}}^{\mathrm{pure}}(\PGL_{3}/F)=\{\chi\otimes\mathrm{St}_{\PGL_{3}},\chi_{\PGL_{3}^{\delta}(F)},\chi_{\PGL_{3}^{\delta^{\prime}}(F)}\},

where
\chi_{\PGL_{3}^{\delta}(F)}
(resp.
\chi_{\PGL_{3}^{\delta^{\prime}}(F)}
) is the character of
\PGL_{3}^{\delta}(F)
(resp.
\PGL_{3}^{\delta^{\prime}}(F)
) obtained by composing
\chi
with the reduced norm.

Table
3.5.2
presents the ABV-packet coefficients for the Langlands parameter
\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3}
.
From Table
3.5.2
we read off the ABV-packets in Case
3.5
.3; the result appears in Table
3.5.1
, where
\mathcal{D}
(resp.
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
) is the character of
A_{\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3d}}
corresponding to the pure inner form
\delta
(resp.
\delta^{\prime}
) in
Z^{1}(F,\PGL_{3})
.

Table 3.5.2.
ABV-packet coefficients
\langle\ ,\ \rangle
for unipotent representations of
\PGL_{3}
and its pure inner forms with infinitesimal parameter
\lambda_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3}
.
The first four rows refer to representations of
\PGL_{3}(F)
, while the fifth and sixth rows are refer to the pure inner forms
\delta
and
\delta^{\prime}
, respectively.
Below, we identify
\delta
(resp.
\delta^{\prime}
) with the character of
A_{\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3d}}
corresponding to the pure inner form
\delta
(resp.
\delta^{\prime}
) in
Z^{1}(F,\PGL_{3})
.
All microlocal fundamental groups are order
3

\begin{array}[]{|c||cccc|}\hline\cr\text{Irreps with inf parameter $\lambda_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3}$}&\widehat{A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3a}}}&\widehat{A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3b}}}&\widehat{A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3c}}}&\widehat{A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3d}}}\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr\chi_{\PGL_{3}(F)}&{\mathbbm{1}}&0&0&0\\
\chi\otimes J_{\alpha_{1}}(\nu\otimes\nu^{-1/2}\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}})&0&{\mathbbm{1}}&0&0\\
\chi\otimes J_{\alpha_{1}}(\nu^{1/2}\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}}\otimes\nu^{-1})&0&0&{\mathbbm{1}}&0\\
\chi\otimes\mathrm{St}_{\PGL_{3}}&0&0&0&{\mathbbm{1}}\\
\chi_{\PGL_{3}^{\delta}(F)}&\delta&\delta&\delta&\delta\\
\chi_{\PGL_{3}^{\delta^{\prime}}(F)}&\delta^{\prime}&\delta^{\prime}&\delta^{\prime}&\delta^{\prime}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

We now explain the calculations behind Table
3.5.2
, as a special case of the techniques of
[
9
]
; see also
[
10
]
.
Let
\lambda_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3}:W_{F}\to\SL_{3}(\mathbb{C})
be the infinitesimal parameter of
\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3d}
:

\lambda_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3}(w){\,:=\,}\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3d}\left(w,\diag({|w|}^{1/2},{|w|}^{-1/2})\right)=\diag({|w|},1,{|w|}^{-1}).

The moduli space of Langlands parameters with infinitesimal parameter
\lambda_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3}
is the direct sum
\mathfrak{u}_{\hat{\alpha}_{1}}\oplus\mathfrak{u}_{\hat{\alpha}_{2}}
of the root space for the simple roots
\hat{\alpha}_{1}
and
\hat{\alpha}_{2}
, equipped with the group action of
{\widehat{T}}
given by
t\cdot(x_{1},x_{2}){\,:=\,}(\hat{\alpha}_{1}(t)x_{1},\hat{\alpha}_{2}(t)x_{2})
.

Vogan’s version of the Langlands correspondence establishes a bijection between simple objects in the category
\mathsf{Per}_{{\widehat{T}}}(\mathfrak{u}_{\hat{\alpha}_{1}}\oplus\mathfrak{u}_{\hat{\alpha}_{2}})
of
{\widehat{T}}
-equivariant perverse sheaves on
\mathfrak{u}_{\hat{\alpha}_{1}}\oplus\mathfrak{u}_{\hat{\alpha}_{2}}
and irreducible admissible representations of
\PGL_{3}
and its pure inner forms with infinitesimal parameter
\lambda_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3}
.
We have already seen there are six of the latter – the four irreducible subquotients of
\text{Ind}_{B_{3}(F)}^{\GL_{3}(F)}(\chi\nu\otimes\chi\otimes\mu\nu^{-1})
together with the characters
\chi_{\PGL_{3}^{\delta}(F)}
and
\chi_{\PGL_{3}^{\delta^{\prime}}(F)}
. Let us enumerate the corresponding simple objects in
\mathsf{Per}_{{\widehat{T}}}(\mathfrak{u}_{\hat{\alpha}_{1}}\oplus\mathfrak{u}_{\hat{\alpha}_{2}})
.
Let
C_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3a}
be the trivial orbit in
V_{\lambda_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3}}
,
let
C_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3b}
be the
{\widehat{T}}
-orbit of
X_{\hat{\alpha}_{1}}
, which is the point in the moduli space for the Langlands parameter
\phi_{1}
for
\chi\otimes J_{\alpha_{1}}(\nu\otimes\nu^{-1/2}\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}})
; let
C_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3c}
be the
{\widehat{T}}
-orbit of
X_{\hat{\alpha}_{2}}
, which is the point in the moduli space for the Langlands parameter
\phi_{2}
for
\chi\otimes J_{\alpha_{1}}(\nu^{1/2}\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}}\otimes\nu^{-1})
.
Finally, let
C_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3d}
be the
{\widehat{T}}
-orbit of
X_{\hat{\alpha}_{1}}+X_{\hat{\alpha}_{2}}
; this orbit of the Langlands parameter
\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3d}
for
\mu\otimes\mathrm{St}_{\PGL_{3}}
.
The equivariant fundamental groups of these orbits are trivial with the exception of the open orbit
C_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3d}
, which has equivariant fundamental group

A_{\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3d}}{\,:=\,}\pi_{1}^{{\widehat{T}}}(C_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3},X_{\hat{\alpha}_{1}}+X_{\hat{\alpha}_{2}})=\pi_{0}(Z_{{\widehat{T}}}(X_{\hat{\alpha}_{1}}+X_{\hat{\alpha}_{2}}))=\{1,\theta_{3},\theta_{3}^{2}\}.

The bijection between
\Pi^{\mathrm{pure}}_{\lambda_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3}}(\PGL_{3}/F)
and the six simple perverse sheaves in
\mathsf{Per}_{{\widehat{T}}}(\mathfrak{u}_{\hat{\alpha}_{1}}\oplus\mathfrak{u}_{\hat{\alpha}_{2}})
are given by the following table.

\begin{array}[]{r|cccc}&C_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3a}&C_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3b}&C_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3c}&C_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3d}\\
\hline\cr\chi_{\PGL_{3}(F)}&{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3a}})&&&\\
\chi\otimes J_{\alpha_{1}}(\nu\otimes\nu^{-1/2}\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}})&&{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3b}})&&\\
\chi\otimes J_{\alpha_{1}}(\nu^{1/2}\mathrm{St}_{\GL_{2}}\otimes\nu^{-1})&&&{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3c}})&\\
\chi\otimes\mathrm{St}_{\PGL_{3}}&&&&{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3d}})\\
\chi_{\PGL_{3}^{\delta}(F)}&&&&{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}(\mathcal{D}_{C_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3d}})\\
\chi_{\PGL_{3}^{\delta^{\prime}}(F)}&&&&{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{C_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3d}}),\end{array}

where
\mathcal{D}
(resp.
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
) is the local system
corresponding to the choice of primitive cube root of unity
\delta
(resp.
\delta^{\prime}
) appearing in the pure inner form
\delta\in Z^{1}(F,\PGL_{3})
.

Now, for each irreducible admissible
\pi
above, and for each Langlands parameter
\phi
with infinitesimal parameter
\lambda_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3}
, we compute
\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}_{C_{\phi}}\mathcal{P}(\pi)
, where
\mathcal{P}(\pi)
is the corresponding simple object in
\mathsf{Per}_{{\widehat{T}}}(\mathfrak{u}_{\hat{\alpha}_{1}}\oplus\mathfrak{u}_{\hat{\alpha}_{2}})
. The result is a local system in
\Lambda_{C_{\phi}}^{\text{sreg}}{\,:=\,}T^{*}_{C_{\phi}}(\mathfrak{u}_{\hat{\alpha}_{1}}\oplus\mathfrak{u}_{\hat{\alpha}_{2}})_{\text{sreg}}
and this may be viewed as a representation of the equivariant fundamental group of
\Lambda_{C_{\phi}}^{\text{sreg}}
, which is
A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
.
The results are summarized in Table
3.5.2
.

The properties in Theorems
2.2
and
2.14
are elementary for
\PGL_{3}(F)
and its pure inner forms as is
[
9
]
*Conjecture 1.

4.
Geometric endoscopy

4.1.
Lifting Langlands parameters

Let
(G,s,\xi)
be an endoscopic triple for
G_{2}
and let
\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G}}
be an unramified Langlands parameter such that
s\in\mathcal{S}^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\xi\circ\phi}
.
The Langlands parameter
\phi
determines, or lifts to, a Langlands parameter
\xi\circ\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G_{2}}}
. Matching the cases that appear in our classification of Langlands parameters for
G
and
G_{2}
is a little complicated, because we have grouped parameters into families based on the geometry created by the corresponding infinitesimal parameters.
Consequently, it happens that different members of these families may lift to different families for
G_{2}
.
To illustrate this phenomenon, here we give the details for one of the six endoscopic groups for
G_{2}
.
Together with all other cases, this information is summarized in Table
4.1.2
.

5

G=\PGL_{3}
,
s={\hat{m}}(\theta_{3}^{2},\theta_{3})
.

(0)

The Langlands parameter
\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.0}
lifts to
\phi_{\ref{infcase:0}}
if and only if
\varphi_{1}({\operatorname{Fr}}),\varphi_{2}({\rm{Fr}}),\varphi_{1}({\rm{Fr}})\varphi_{2}({\rm{Fr}})\notin\{q^{\pm 1}\}
; lifts to
\phi_{\ref{infcase:1-short}a}
if and only if one of
\varphi_{1}({\operatorname{Fr}}),\varphi_{2}({\rm{Fr}}),\varphi_{1}({\rm{Fr}})\varphi_{2}({\rm{Fr}})
is in
\{q^{\pm 1}\}
; and lifts to
\phi_{\ref{infcase:5}a}
if and only if two of
\varphi_{1}({\operatorname{Fr}}),\varphi_{2}({\rm{Fr}}),\varphi_{1}({\rm{Fr}})\varphi_{2}({\rm{Fr}})
are in
\{q^{\pm 1}\}
.

(1)

The Langlands parameter
\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.1a}
(resp.
\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.1a}
) lifts to
\phi_{\ref{infcase:2-long}a}
(resp.
\phi_{\ref{infcase:2-long}b}
) if and only if
\varphi({\rm{Fr}})\notin\{-q^{\pm 1/2},q^{\pm 3/2}\}
;
\phi_{\ref{infcase:4-D2}a}
(resp.
\phi_{\ref{infcase:4-D2}b}
) if and only if
\varphi({\rm{Fr}})\in\{-q^{\pm 1/2}\}
;
\phi_{\ref{infcase:7-reg}a}
(resp.
\phi_{\ref{infcase:7-reg}b}
) if and only if
\varphi({\rm{Fr}})\in\{q^{\pm 3/2}\}
.

(2)

The Langlands parameter
\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.2a}
(resp.
\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.2b}
) lifts to
\phi_{\ref{infcase:3}a}
(resp.
\phi_{\ref{infcase:3}b}
).

(3)

The Langlands parameter
\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3a}
(resp.
\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3b}
,
\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3c}
,
\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3d}
) lifts to
\phi_{\ref{infcase:6-A2}a}
(resp.
\phi_{\ref{infcase:6-A2}b}
,
\phi_{\ref{infcase:6-A2}b}
,
\phi_{\ref{infcase:6-A2}b}
) if and only if
\varphi({\rm{Fr}})\neq 1
; and lifts to
\phi_{\ref{infcase:8-sub}a}
(resp.
\phi_{\ref{infcase:8-sub}b}
,
\phi_{\ref{infcase:8-sub}c}
,
\phi_{\ref{infcase:8-sub}d}
) if and only if
\varphi({\rm{Fr}})=1
.

If we turn the problem of lifting parameters on its head, our classification of scheme for Langlands parameters is more illuminating: instead of lifting parameters from endoscopic groups, begin with a Langlands parameter for
G_{2}
and find all endoscopic triples
(G,s,\xi)
such that the Langlands parameter factors through
\xi:{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G}}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G_{2}}}
. This information appears in Table
4.1.1
.

. The column "Endoscopic Langlands parameters" refers to Sections
3.2
through
3.4
.
The column "Arthur-type" indicates those
\xi
-conormal parameters that, when unitary, are parameters of Arthur type; see Definition
6
for this notion.
The lifts appearing in the column "other regular" indicates all other
\xi
-conormal parameters.
In these last two cases, the information in these two columns refers to the classification of unramified Langlands parameters for
G_{2}
appearing in Section
1.2
.
Finally, the column "irregular lifts" indicates all remaining lifts.

4.2.
Regular conormal vectors and Arthur parameters

Let
(G,s,\xi)
be an endoscopic triple for
G_{2}
and let
\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G}}
be an unramified Langlands parameter.
Let
\lambda
be the infinitesimal parameter for
\phi
; then
\xi\circ\lambda
is the infinitesimal parameter for
\xi\circ\phi
.
Now consider the map
\xi:{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G}}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G_{2}}}
on dual groups and the induced homomorphism
{\widehat{G}}\to{\widehat{G_{2}}}
, also denoted by
\xi
below. Pass to tangent spaces
d\xi:{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\to{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}}
and restricted to Vogan varieties to define the embedding

d\xi|_{V_{\lambda}}:V_{\lambda}\to V_{\xi\circ\lambda}

such that the subvariety
V_{\lambda}
is the fixed-point set of the
(V_{\xi\circ\lambda})^{s}=V_{\lambda}
. This induces

d\xi|_{T^{*}(V_{\lambda})}:T^{*}(V_{\lambda})\to T^{*}(V_{\xi\circ\lambda})

such that
\left(T^{*}(V_{\xi\circ\lambda})\right)^{s}=T^{*}(V_{\lambda})
; recall that we may view
T^{*}(V_{\lambda})
as a subvariety of
{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}
. Likewise, this induces
a map of conormal bundles

d\xi|_{\Lambda_{\lambda}}:\Lambda_{\lambda}\to\Lambda_{\xi\circ\lambda}

such that
\Lambda_{\lambda}=\left(\Lambda_{\xi\circ\lambda}\right)^{s}
; again, we may view
\Lambda_{\lambda}
as a subvariety of
{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}
.
Now let
x_{\phi}
(resp.
x_{\xi\circ\phi}
) be the point in the moduli space
V_{\lambda}
(resp.
V_{\xi\circ\lambda}
) for
\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G}}
(resp. for
\xi\circ\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G_{2}}}
) and let
C_{\phi}
(resp.
C_{\xi\circ\phi}
) be its
H_{\lambda}
-orbit (resp.
H_{\xi\circ\lambda}
-orbit).
Now
d\xi|_{\Lambda_{\lambda}}
restricts to an immersion

d\xi|_{\Lambda_{\lambda,C_{\phi}}}:\Lambda_{\lambda,C_{\phi}}\to\Lambda_{\xi\circ\lambda,C_{\xi\circ\phi}}.
Now consider the restriction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda_{\lambda,C_{\phi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda_{\xi\circ\lambda,C_{\xi\circ\phi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda_{\lambda,C_{\phi}}^{\text{sreg}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d\xi|_{\Lambda_{\lambda,C_{\phi}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d\xi|_{\Lambda_{\lambda,C_{\phi}}^{\text{sreg}}}}

Definition 6
.

Let
(G,s,\xi)
be an endoscopic triple for
G_{2}
and let
\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G}}
be an unramified Langlands parameter.
Let us say that a Langlands parameter
\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G}}
is
\xi
-conormal, if the image of
d\xi|_{\Lambda_{\lambda,C_{\phi}}^{\text{sreg}}}
is contained in
\Lambda_{\xi\circ\lambda,C_{\xi\circ\phi}}^{\text{sreg}}
.
If
\phi
is
\xi
-conormal the map

d\xi|_{\Lambda_{\lambda,C_{\phi}}^{\text{sreg}}}:{\Lambda_{\lambda,C_{\phi}}^{\text{sreg}}}\to\Lambda_{\xi\circ\lambda,C_{\xi\circ\phi}}^{\text{sreg}}

induces a group homomorphism of
equivariant fundamental groups, denoted by

d\xi_{\phi}:A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}\to A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\xi\circ\phi}.

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
(G,s,\xi)
be an endoscopic triple for
G_{2}
and let
\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G}}
be an unramified Langlands parameter.
If
\phi
is of Arthur type then
\phi
is
\xi
-conormal.

Proof.

Let
\psi:W^{\prime\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G}}
be the Arthur parameter such that
\phi(w,x)=\psi(w,x,d_{w})
.
Set
x{\,:=\,}d\psi(1,e,1)
and
y{\,:=\,}d\psi(1,1,f)
.
Then by
[
9
]
*Prop. 6.1.1 we have
(x,y)
is a strongly regular conormal vector for
\phi
.
Consider
\xi\circ\psi:W^{\prime\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G_{2}}}
. Then again by
[
9
]
*Prop. 6.1.1
we have
d\xi(x,y)=(d(\xi\circ\psi)(1,e,1),d(\xi\circ\psi)(1,1,f))
is a strongly regular conormal vector for
\xi\circ\phi
.
∎

In Table
4.1.2
we present a complete list of all endoscopic triples
(G,s,\xi)
and all unramified Langlands parameters
\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G_{2}}}
for which
\phi
is
\xi
-conormal. We now explain this table.
The left-hand column of Table
4.1.2
lists the endoscopic groups
G
for
G_{2}
as explained in Section
3.1
. Then, for each such
G
, we list the families of endoscopic Langlands parameters
\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G}}
as they appeared in Sections
3.2
through
3.4
.
In Section
4.1
we saw that for each
\phi
in these families, there are multiple Langlands parameters for
G_{2}(F)
to which
\phi
may lift, depending on the properties of
\phi
. Each row of Table
4.1.2
lists the lifts
\xi\circ\phi
that arise as
\phi
ranges through each family, partially ordered left to right along rows by the relative dimension
\dim(\xi\circ\phi)-\dim(\phi)
; note that if this relative dimension is
0
then
\phi
is trivially
\xi
-conormal.

4.3.
Statement of the fixed-point formula

Theorem 4.2
.

Let
(G,s,\xi)
be an endoscopic triple for
G_{2}
and let
\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G}}
be an unramified Langlands parameter for
G
.
If
\phi
is
\xi
-conormal then

\trace_{s}\left(\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}_{\xi\circ\phi}[\dim(\xi\circ\phi)]\mathcal{P}\right)=\trace_{s}\left(\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}_{\phi}[\dim(\phi)]\ {\operatorname{res}}\mathcal{P}\right)

for every
\mathcal{P}\in\mathsf{Per}_{H_{\xi\circ\lambda}}(V_{\xi\circ\lambda})
.

The proof of Theorem
4.2
will occupy the rest of this Section, so we explain the strategy here.
We work through the list of endoscopic triples
(G,s,\xi)
for
G_{2}
and, in each case, we recall that classification of Langlands parameters
\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G}}
in terms of infinitesimal parameters in Sections
3.2
through
3.4
.
We identify those Langlands parameters
\phi
that are
\xi
-conormal, expanding on the information presented in Table
4.1.2
. We then recall the classification of Vogan varieties
H_{\xi\circ\lambda}\times V_{\xi\circ\lambda}\to V_{\xi\circ\lambda}
in terms of prehomogeneous vector spaces
H\times V\to V
from Section
1.2
and we observe that same list of five prehomogeneous vector spaces capture all instances of the Vogan varieties
H_{\lambda}\times V_{\lambda}\to V_{\lambda}
. We further show that the embeddings

d\xi|_{V_{\lambda}}:V_{\lambda}\to V_{\xi\circ\lambda},

explained at the beginning of Section
4.2
, all take the form

V^{s}\subseteq V

where
V
is one of these five prehomogeneous vector spaces and
s\in H
has finite order (hence semisimple).
We are then able to match
L
-parameters
\phi
with
H^{s}
-orbits
C^{\prime}\subseteq V^{s}
and identify those that are
\xi
-conormal with the corresponding property of the
H^{s}
-orbit
C^{\prime}
; we refer to these orbits as
V
-conormal. This is the genesis of the notion of
\xi
-conormal, in fact.
We then prove the theorem for these prehomogeneous vector spaces in the form of Proposition
4.4
, for all
V
-conormal orbits
C^{\prime}\subseteq V^{s}
.

4.4.
Prehomogeneous vector subspaces

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
H\times V\to V
be one of the five prehomogenous vector spaces appearing in Proposition
1.2
; observe that
H
is reductive in these cases.
For semisimple
s\in H
, set
H^{s}{\,:=\,}Z_{H}(s)
and
V^{s}{\,:=\,}\{x\in V{\ |\ }s(x)=x\}
.
Then
H^{s}\times V^{s}\to V^{s}
be is also a prehomogeneous vector space and again one of the five prehomogenous vector spaces appearing in Proposition
1.2
.

Proof.

When
V=V^{s}
and
H=H^{s}
this is a tautology, whereas
V=V^{s}
and
H\neq H^{s}
only occurs for
1
.

Up to the natural notion of equivalence, the possibilities for proper fix-point prehomogenous vector subspaces are the following.

2

The prehomogeneous vector space
V=\mathbb{A}^{1}
with
H=\GL_{1}
-action (scalar multiplication) has only one proper fixed-point prehomogeneous vector subspace:

(i)

V^{s}=\{0\}
with
H^{s}=\GL_{1}
(this is
1
);

3

The prehomogeneous vector space
V=\mathbb{A}^{2}
with
H=\GL_{2}
-action given by twisted matrix multiplication
h.x=\det(h)^{n}\,hx
also has the following proper fixed-point prehomogeneous vector subspaces:

(i)

V^{s}=\{0\}
with
H^{s}=\GL_{2}
or
\GL_{1}^{2}
(this is
1
);

(ii)

V^{s}=\{(x_{1},0){\ |\ }x_{1}\}
and
H^{s}=\GL_{1}^{2}
(this is equivalent to
2
);

4

The prehomogeneous vector space
V=\mathbb{A}^{2}
with
H=\GL_{1}^{2}
-action given by
(t_{1},t_{2}).(x_{1},x_{2})=(t_{1}x_{1},t_{1}t_{2}^{n}x_{2})
, for positive integer
n
, the following proper fixed-point prehomogeneous vector subspaces are possible:

(i)

V^{s}=\{(0,0)\}
and
H^{s}=H
(this is
1
);

(ii)

V^{s}=\{(x_{1},0){\ |\ }x_{1}\}
and
H^{s}=H
(this is
2
);

(iii)

V^{s}=\{(0,x_{2}){\ |\ }x_{2}\}
and
H^{s}=H
(this is
2
);

5

The prehomogeneous vector space
V=\mathbb{A}^{4}
with
H=\GL_{2}
and action
\det^{-1}\otimes\Sym^{3}
has the following proper fixed-point prehomogeneous vector subspaces, up to isomorphism:

(i)

V^{s}=\{(0,0,0,0)\}
and
H^{s}=\GL_{2}
or
\GL_{1}^{2}
(this is
1
);

(ii)

V^{s}=\{(x_{1},0,0,0){\ |\ }x_{1}\}
and
H^{s}=\GL_{1}^{2}
(this is equivalent to
2
);

(iii)

V^{s}=\{(0,x_{2},0,0){\ |\ }x_{2}\}
and
H^{s}=\GL_{1}^{2}
(this is equivalent to
2
);

(iv)

V^{s}=\{(x_{1},0,x_{3},0){\ |\ }x_{1},x_{3}\}
and
H^{s}=\GL_{1}^{2}
(this is
4
for
n=2
);

(v)

V^{s}=\{(x_{1},0,0,x_{4}){\ |\ }x_{1},x_{4}\}
and
H^{s}=\GL_{1}^{2}
(this is
4
for
n=3
).
∎

4.5.
V
-conormal

Let
H\times V\to V
be one of the prehomogeneous vector spaces appearing in Proposition
1.2
and let
H^{s}\times V^{s}\to V^{s}
be one of the subspaces appearing in Lemma
4.3
, for semisimple
s\in H
.
Then
T^{*}(V)^{s}=T^{*}(V^{s})
, where
H
acts on
T^{*}(V)
by
s\cdot(x,y){\,:=\,}(s\cdot x,s\cdot y)
. Recall that
\Lambda=\{(x,y)\in T^{*}(V){\ |\ }[x,y]=0\}
; then
\Lambda^{s}=\{(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\in T^{*}(V^{s}){\ |\ }[x^{\prime},y^{\prime}]=0\}
.

Now let
C^{\prime}\subseteq V^{s}
be an
H^{s}
-orbit.
The
H
-orbit of
C^{\prime}
, denoted by
H\cdot C^{\prime}\subseteq V
and sometimes called the saturation of
C^{\prime}
, is a single
H
-orbit, denoted below by
C
.
The intersection
C^{s}
of
C
with
V^{s}
contains
C^{\prime}
but may contain other
H^{s}
-orbits: we write
C^{s}=\cup_{i}C^{\prime}_{i}
.
Then,
(\Lambda_{C})^{s}=\cup_{i}(\Lambda^{s})_{C^{\prime}_{i}}

Passing to the regular part of the conormal varieties we find that it can happen that

(\Lambda^{\text{sreg}}_{C})^{s}
and
\cup_{i}(\Lambda^{s}_{C_{i}^{\prime}})^{\text{sreg}}
are not equal. Of course, this is essentially the same phenomenon discussed in Section
4.2
. Definition
6
now takes this form.

Definition 7
.

With notation as above, let us say that an
H^{s}
-orbit
C^{\prime}\subseteq V^{s}
is
V
-conormal if
(\Lambda^{s}_{C^{\prime}})^{\text{sreg}}\subseteq(\Lambda^{\text{sreg}}_{C})^{s}
, where the
H
-orbit
C\subseteq V
is the saturation of
C^{\prime}
.
In this case, this inclusion induces a map of equivariant fundamental groups
A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{C^{\prime}}\to A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{C}
.

Whenever
V=V^{s}
and
H=H^{s}
then we trivially have that every orbit is
V
-conormal.
For those cases under consideration in this paper where
V=V^{s}
and
H\neq H^{s}
the same remains true. We note that this only occurs for
1
.

From the cases of prehomogenous vector spaces
V
and proper subspaces
V^{s}
appearing in Lemma
4.3
, only the following
H^{s}
-orbits
C^{\prime}\subset V^{s}
are
V
-conormal:

(1)

Case
3
(ii).
The closed orbit
C^{\prime}_{0}
and the open orbit
C^{\prime}_{1}
in
V^{s}
are
V
-conormal.
We note that the saturation of the closed
H^{s}
-orbit
C^{\prime}_{0}
in
V^{s}
is the closed
H
-orbit
C_{0}
in
V
; the saturation of the open
H^{s}
-orbit
C^{\prime}_{1}
in
V^{s}
is the
H
-orbit
C_{1}
in
V
, which is open.

(2)

Case
5
(iv).
Every
H^{s}
-orbit
C^{\prime}
in
V^{s}
is
V
-conormal.
These orbits are denoted by
C^{\prime}_{0}
,
C^{\prime}_{1}
,
C^{\prime}_{2}
and
C^{\prime}_{3}
, where
C^{\prime}_{0}
is closed and
C^{\prime}_{3}
is open and where
C^{\prime}_{1}
is the
H^{s}
-orbit of
(1,0,0,0)
and
C^{\prime}_{2}
is the
H^{s}
-orbit of
(0,0,1,0)
.
The saturation of the closed
H^{s}
-orbit
C^{\prime}_{0}
is the closed
H
-orbit
C_{0}
, the saturation of
C^{\prime}_{1}
is
C_{1}
, the saturation of
C^{\prime}_{2}
is
C_{2}
and the saturation of the open
H^{s}
-orbit
C^{\prime}_{3}
is the open
H
-orbit
C_{3}
.

(3)

Case
5
(v).
Only two of the four
H^{s}
-orbits
C^{\prime}
in
V^{s}
are
V
-conormal.
Let the
H^{s}
-orbits in
V^{s}
be denoted by
C^{\prime}_{0}
,
C^{\prime}_{1}
,
C^{\prime}_{2}
and
C^{\prime}_{3}
, where
C^{\prime}_{0}
is closed and
C^{\prime}_{3}
is open and where
C^{\prime}_{1}
is the
H^{s}
-orbit of
(1,0,0,0)
while
C^{\prime}_{2}
is the
H^{s}
-orbit of
(0,0,0,1)
.
Then the saturation of the closed
H^{s}
-orbit
C^{\prime}_{0}
is the closed
H
-orbit
C_{0}
, the saturation of
C^{\prime}_{1}
is
C_{1}
, the saturation of
C^{\prime}_{2}
is
C_{1}
again, and the saturation of the open
H^{s}
-orbit
C^{\prime}_{3}
is the open
H
-orbit
C_{3}
.
Only
C^{\prime}_{0}
and
C^{\prime}_{3}
are
V
-conormal.

4.6.
Restriction

Let
(G,s,\xi)
be an endscopic triple for
G_{2}
over
F
.
Let
\lambda
be an infinitesimal parameter for
G
.
To prove Theorem
4.2
we will calculate the equivariant restriction functor

{\operatorname{res}}:D_{H_{\lambda}}(V_{\xi\circ\lambda})\to D_{H_{\lambda}}(V_{\lambda})

on simple objects in the abelian category
\mathsf{Per}_{H}(V_{\xi\circ\lambda})
. As we will see, the restriction
{\operatorname{res}}\,\mathcal{P}
of an equivariant perverse sheaf is not necessarily perverse.
To address this issue, we replace
\mathsf{Per}_{H}(V_{\lambda})
with the full subcategory
\mathsf{Per}_{H}^{\bullet}(V_{\lambda})
of
D_{H}(V_{\lambda})
generated by shifts
\mathcal{P}[n]
of equivariant perverse sheaves. In this section we will see that
{\operatorname{res}}
defines

{\operatorname{res}}:\mathsf{Per}^{\bullet}_{H_{\xi\circ\lambda}}(V_{\xi\circ\lambda})\to\mathsf{Per}^{\bullet}_{H_{\lambda}}(V_{\lambda})

We use this fact to replace restriction along
d\xi:V_{\lambda}\to V_{\xi\circ\lambda}
with restriction along
V^{s}\hookrightarrow V
, where
H\times V\to V
is one of the five prehomogeneous vector spaces from Proposition
1.2
and
s\in H
is semisimple.
The functor

{\operatorname{res}}:\mathsf{Per}^{\bullet}_{H}(V)\to\mathsf{Per}^{\bullet}_{H^{s}}(V^{s})

on simple objects is given in Table
4.6.1
for the three prehomogeneous vector spaces appearing semisimple
s\in H
appearing in Section
4.5
.

In Table
4.6.1
we find four indecomposable perverse sheaves, given here.

•

On
4
(for
n=2
) we define
\mathcal{F}_{2}={\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{2}\cup C_{1}\cup C_{0}}[1]
which is cohomologically equivalent to a complex

\cdots\rightarrow 0\rightarrow({\mathbbm{1}}_{\overline{C_{2}}}\oplus{\mathbbm{1}}_{\overline{C_{1}}})\rightarrow{\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{0}}

and hence sits in the exact sequence

0\rightarrow{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{0}})\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{2}\rightarrow{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{1}})\oplus{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{2}})\rightarrow 0,

which we use to compute the functor
\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}
on these perverse sheaves in Table
4.7.2
.

•

On
4
(for
n=2
) we define
\mathcal{F}_{3}={\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{3}\cup C_{2}}[2]
which is cohomologically equivalent to a complex

\cdots\rightarrow 0\rightarrow{\mathbbm{1}}_{\overline{C_{3}}}\rightarrow 1_{\overline{C_{1}}}\rightarrow 0

and hence sits in the exact sequence

0\rightarrow{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{1}})\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{3}\rightarrow{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{3}})\rightarrow 0,

which we use to compute the functor
\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}
on these perverse sheaves in Table
4.7.2
.

4
(for
n=2
) we define
\mathcal{F}_{4}={\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{2}\cup C_{1}\cup C_{0}}[1]
which is cohomologically equivalent to a complex

\cdots\rightarrow 0\rightarrow({\mathbbm{1}}_{\overline{C_{2}}}\oplus{\mathbbm{1}}_{\overline{C_{1}}})\rightarrow{\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{0}}

and hence sits in the exact sequence

0\rightarrow{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{0}})\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{4}\rightarrow{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{1}})\oplus{\mathcal{I\hskip-1.0ptC}}({\mathbbm{1}}_{C_{2}})\rightarrow 0,

which we use to compute the functor

On
4
(for
n=2

.

4.7.
Proof of Theorem
4.2

Let
H\times V\to V
be one of the prehomogeneous vector spaces appearing in Proposition
1.2
.
The result is immediate if
V=V^{s}
and
H=H^{s}
or in the case
1
so we
let
H^{s}\times V^{s}\to V^{s}
be one of the proper prehomogeneous vector spaces appearing in Lemma
4.3
.
Let
C^{\prime}
be an
H^{s}
-orbit in
V^{s}
and let
C\subseteq V
be the saturation of
C^{\prime}
.
In Proposition
4.4
we show that if
C^{\prime}
is
V
-conormal then the outside of the following diagram commutes.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{H}(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{H}(\Lambda^{\text{sreg}}_{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{H}((\Lambda^{\text{sreg}}_{C})^{s})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{H^{s}}(V^{s})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{H^{s}}((\Lambda^{s}_{C^{\prime}})^{\text{sreg}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces res}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}_{C}[\dim C]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{res}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\trace_{s}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{res}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}_{C^{\prime}}[\dim C^{\prime}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\trace_{s}}

It is important to note that the square in this diagram does not commute.

Proposition 4.4
.

Let
H\times V\to V
be one of the prehomogeneous vector spaces appearing in Proposition
1.2
and let
H^{s}\times V^{s}\to V^{s}
be one of the prehomogeneous vector spaces appearing in Lemma
4.3
.
Let
C^{\prime}
be an
H^{s}
-orbit in
V^{s}
.
If
C^{\prime}
is
V
-conormal then

(13)

\trace_{s}\left(\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}_{C}[\dim C]\ \mathcal{P}\right)=\trace_{s}\left(\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}_{C^{\prime}}[\dim C^{\prime}]{\operatorname{res}}\,\mathcal{P}|_{V^{s}}\right),

for all equivariant perverse sheaves
\mathcal{P}
on
V
, where
C\subseteq V
is the saturation of
C^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We prove this by verifying Equation (
13
) by calculating the left- and right-hand sides of Equation (
13
) independently, in each of the three cases appearing in Section
4.5
.

(1)

Case
3
(ii).
The main calculations are summarized by the Table
4.7.1
.
The calculations are completed by comparing
\trace_{s}
of the sheaves in the final two columns.
In this case these are always
1
.
We note that for each
C_{i}^{\prime}
the saturation is
C_{i}
.

(iv).
This is the fixed-point prehomogeneous vector subspace

for

and

in the case

.

; let

be the corresponding local system on

.

The main calculations are summarized by the Table
4.7.2
.
The calculations are completed by comparing

s
in

,

,
\trace_{s}\rho=-1
and
\trace_{s}\vartheta_{2}=-1
. We note that for each
C_{i}^{\prime}
the saturation is
C_{i}
.

).∎

4.8.
Geometric lifting of stable distributions

From Section
3
, recall Definition
5
:

\Theta_{\phi,s}^{G^{\delta}}{\,:=\,}e(\delta)\sum_{\pi\in\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}(G^{\delta}(F))}\hskip 0.0pt\trace_{s}\left(\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}_{\phi}[\dim(\phi)]\mathcal{P}(\pi)[-\dim(\pi)]\right)\ \Theta_{\pi},

where
(G,s,\xi)
is an endoscopic triple for
G_{2}
and
\delta\in Z^{1}(G,F)
is a pure inner form of
G
.
Recall also that this distribution can be written in the form

\Theta_{\phi,s}^{G^{\delta}}{\,:=\,}e(\delta)\sum_{\pi\in\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}(G^{\delta}(F))}(-1)^{\dim(\phi)-\dim(\pi)}\langle s,\pi\rangle\ \Theta_{\pi}.

Remark 4.5
.

If the Langlands parameter
\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G}}
is not relevant to
G(F)
, in the sense of
[
6
]
, then
\Theta_{\phi,s}^{G^{\delta}}
may be trivial; for example,

\Theta_{\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.0}}^{\PGL_{3}^{\delta}}=0

for non-trivial
\delta
. On the other hand,
\Theta_{\phi,s}^{G^{\delta}}
can be non-trivial even when
\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G}}
is not relevant to
G(F)
; for example, although
\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3a}
is not relevant to
{\PGL_{3}^{\delta}}
for non-trivial
\delta
,

\Theta_{\phi_{\ref{ssec:PGL3}.3a},s}^{\PGL_{3}^{\delta}}=e(\delta)\Theta_{\chi_{\PGL_{3}^{\delta}(F)}},

which is non-trivial.

Definition 8
.

Let
(G,s,\xi)
be an endoscopic triple for
G_{2}
.
Let
\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G}}
be an unramified Langlands parameter.
We define

\Lift_{(G,s,\xi)}\Theta_{\phi}^{G}{\,:=\,}\sum_{\pi\in\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\xi\circ\phi}(G_{2}(F))}\hskip-20.0pt\trace_{s}\left(\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}_{\phi}[\dim(\phi)]\ {\operatorname{res}}\,\mathcal{P}(\pi)[-\dim(\pi)]\right)\ \Theta_{\pi},

where
{\operatorname{res}}:D_{Z_{\widehat{G_{2}}}(\xi\circ\phi)}(V_{\xi\circ\lambda})\to D_{Z_{\widehat{G}}(\phi)}(V_{\lambda})
is the equivariant functor defined by
{\operatorname{res}}\,\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}|_{V_{\lambda}}
.
If the context make it clear what the endoscopic triple is, we will sometimes use the abbreviated notation
\Lift_{G}^{G_{2}}
for
\Lift_{(G,s,\xi)}
.
Let
\delta\in Z^{1}(F,G)
be a pure inner form of
G
.
Then
(G,1,\id)
is an endoscopic triple for
G^{\delta}
over
F
and, extending the definition above,

\Lift_{G}^{G^{\delta}}\Theta_{\phi}^{G}{\,:=\,}e(\delta)\mathop{\sum}\limits_{\pi\in\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\xi\circ\phi}(G^{\delta}(F))}\trace_{1}\left(\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}_{\phi}[\dim(\phi)]\ {\operatorname{res}}\,\mathcal{P}(\pi)[-\dim(\pi)]\right)\ \Theta_{\pi}.

Conjecture 1
.

Let
(G,s,\xi)
be an endoscopic triple for
G_{2}
and let
\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G}}
be a unramified Langlands parameter that is
\xi
-conormal.
The Langlands-Shelstad transfer of
\Theta^{G}_{\phi}
from
G(F)
to
G_{2}(F)
is
\Theta_{\xi\circ\phi,s}^{G_{2}}
.
Now let
\delta\in Z^{1}(F,G)
be a pure inner form for
G
over
F
and suppose
\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G}}
is also relevant to
G^{\delta}(F)
.
Then the Jacquet-Langlands transfer of
\Theta^{G}_{\phi}
from
G(F)
to
G^{\delta}(F)
is
e(\delta)\Theta_{\phi}^{G^{\delta}}

Theorem 4.6
.

Let
(G,s,\xi)
be an endoscopic triple for
G_{2}
.
Let
\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G}}
be a unramified Langlands parameter that is
\xi
-conormal.
Then

\Lift_{(G,s,\xi)}^{G}\Theta^{G}_{\phi}=\Theta_{\xi\circ\phi,s}.

Let
\delta\in Z^{1}(F,G)
be a pure inner form of
G
and suppose also that
\phi
is relevant to
G^{\delta}
. Then

\Lift_{G}^{G^{\delta}}\Theta_{\phi}^{G}=e(\delta)\Theta_{\phi}^{G^{\delta}}.

Proof.

Theorem
4.2
, we have

(14)

\trace_{s}\left(\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}_{\phi}[\dim(\phi)]\ \mathcal{P}\right)=\trace_{s}\left(\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}_{\xi\circ\phi}[\dim(\xi\circ\phi)]{\operatorname{res}}\mathcal{P}\right),

for every simple object
\mathcal{P}
in
\mathsf{Per}_{Z_{\widehat{G_{2}}}(\xi\circ\lambda)}(V_{\xi\circ\lambda})
.
Thus,

\begin{array}[]{rcl r}\Lift_{(G,s,\xi)}\Theta^{G}_{\phi}&:=&\mathop{\sum}\limits_{\pi\in\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\xi\circ\phi}(G_{2}(F))}\hskip 0.0pt\trace_{s}\left(\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}_{\phi}[\dim(\phi)]{\operatorname{res}}\,\mathcal{P}(\pi)[-\dim(\pi)]\right)\ \Theta_{\pi}&\\
&=&\mathop{\sum}\limits_{\pi\in\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\xi\circ\phi}(G_{2}(F))}\hskip 0.0pt\trace_{s}\left(\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}_{\xi\circ\phi}[\dim(\xi\circ\phi)]\mathcal{P}(\pi)[-\dim(\pi)]\right)\ \Theta_{\pi}&\\
&=:&\Theta_{\xi\circ\phi,s}.&\end{array}

Directly from the definitions we see

\begin{array}[b]{rcl}\Lift_{G}^{G^{\delta}}\Theta_{\phi}^{G}&{\,:=\,}&e(\delta)\sum_{\pi\in\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\xi\circ\phi}(G^{\delta}(F))}\trace_{1}\left(\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}_{\phi}[\dim(\phi)]\ {\operatorname{res}}\,\mathcal{P}(\pi)[-\dim(\pi)]\right)\ \Theta_{\pi}\\
&=&e(\delta)\sum_{\pi\in\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\xi\circ\phi}(G^{\delta}(F))}\trace_{1}\left(\operatorname{\mathsf{N}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{E}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{v}\hskip-1.0pt\mathsf{s}}_{\phi}[\dim(\phi)]\ \mathcal{P}(\pi)[-\dim(\pi)]\right)\ \Theta_{\pi}\\
&=&e(\delta)\Theta_{\phi}^{G^{\delta}}.\end{array}\qed

Definition 9
.

Let
\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G_{2}}}
be an unramified Langlands parameter for
G_{2}(F)
.
If
s\in\mathcal{S}^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
then
\phi=\xi\circ\phi^{s}
for an endoscopic triple
(G,s,\xi)
and a Langlands parameter
\phi^{s}:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G}}
.
If
\phi
has the property that
\phi^{s}
is
\xi
-conormal, we say that
\phi
is
s
-conormal.
If
\phi
is of Arthur type then
\phi
is
s
-conormal for every
s\in\mathcal{S}^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
.

Theorem 4.7
.

Let
\phi:W^{\prime}_{F}\to{\hskip-2.0pt\,{}^{L}\hskip-1.0pt{G_{2}}}
be an unramified Langlands parameter that is
s
-conormal for every
s\in\mathcal{S}^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
.
If
\pi
is any unipotent representation of
G_{2}(F)
then the distribution character
\Theta_{\pi}
may be expressed as a linear combination of the distributions
\Lift^{G_{2}}_{(G,s,\xi)}\Theta^{G}_{\phi^{s}}
, letting
\phi
range over Langlands parameters with the same infinitesimal parameter as
\pi
and letting
s
range over
\mathcal{S}^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
.
And if
\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}(G_{2}(F))\to\widehat{A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}}
is a bijection then,

\Theta_{\pi}=\sum_{(G,s,\xi)}(-1)^{\dim(\phi^{s})-\dim(\pi)}\frac{\overline{\langle s,\pi\rangle}}{{|Z_{A_{\phi}}(s)|}}\ \Lift_{(G,s,\xi)}\Theta^{G}_{\phi^{s}},\qquad\qquad\forall\pi\in\Pi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}(G_{2}(F)),

where the sum is taken over equivalence classes of endoscopic triples
(G,s,\xi)
with
s\in\mathcal{S}^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
and where we identify
s
with its image under
\mathcal{S}^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}\to A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
in the calculation of
Z_{A_{\phi}}(s)
.

Proof.

Distribution characters for equivalence classes of irreducible admissible representations of
G_{2}(F)
are linearly independent by
[
5
]
. For each unramified Langlands parameter
\phi
for
G_{2}(F)
, the number of equivalence classes of endoscopic triples for
(G,s,\xi)
for
G_{2}(F)
with
s\in\mathcal{S}^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
is equal to the number of irreducible representations of
A^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{\scalebox{0.5}{$\mathrm{ABV}$}}}}_{\phi}
. This makes it possible to determine the characters
\langle s,\pi\rangle
appearing in the sum
\Theta_{\phi,s}=\sum_{\pi}(-1)^{\dim(\phi)-\dim(\pi)}\langle s,\pi\rangle\ \Theta_{\pi}
. The rest of the theorem is a direct consequence of
1
.
∎
